# STATUS — where the project stands

Living document 4 of 4 (budget: `PLAN.md` §7).  Decisions live in `PLAN.md` §2, the architecture in
`ARCHITECTURE.md`, the frames in `FRAMES.md`.  This file is only: what is done, with evidence, and what is
next.

**ONE MISSION, ONE PIPELINE.**  Mission A — the 2.0 x 1.2 m square in `worlds/pillars.yaml`, flown by
`planner/rectangle_source.py` + `fsm/mission_fsm.py` with **no planner process in the loop** — is the
working pipeline.  The ego-planner line (mission B) was DROPPED by the owner on 2026-10-15 and removed from
the tree the same day; the last section of this file is what it taught us, and nothing above it depends on
it.

## Done, with evidence

Rows 1-30 are the stack itself (S3-S7 of `PLAN.md` §5).  Where a row reports a number, the run directory or
the metrics file that carries it is named.

With no GPS, no compass and no vision the EKF cannot observe yaw -- which is why the mission feeds it the LIO |
| 22 | **S6: the clean upstream LIO runs end to end on our simulated sensor** | built from `f89f48d`; `liblio.so` md5 `e4b82a73cbfb70a7a538514f74936b09`; fed by `/livox/lidar` + `/livox/imu` it publishes `/lio/odom` at **10.02 Hz** and `/lio/robo/odom` at **199.26 Hz**.  Parked **40 s**: drift max excursion **26.60 mm**, rms **9.43 mm** |
| 23 | a measurement that changed the sensor model: a noiseless IMU made the test meaningless | with the IMU as first written (exact truth, no noise) the LIO tracked to **0.01 mm** parked -- the estimator was being tested on a problem with no noise in it.  `sim/lidar_imu.py` now injects the measured `imu_ng 0.005` / `imu_na 0.1` plus a bias and 10 mm lidar range noise; `--no-noise` keeps the A/B |
| 24 | **S7: the FCU flies on external navigation alone -- no GPS, no compass** | `tools/run-fcu-link-test.sh` + `tools/test-fcu-link.py`, feeding the truth tap straight to the FCU as `ODOMETRY`: **armed**, **GUIDED takeoff ACCEPTED**, truth altitude **1.24 m**, `landed_state=2 (IN_AIR)`, motors modulated.  Statustext: "EKF3 IMU0 is using external nav data" |
| 25 | S7: the traps between "connected" and "flying", each measured | (1) `--defaults` twice does NOT accumulate -- the second REPLACES the first; (2) a fresh EEPROM needs `FRAME_CLASS`/`FRAME_TYPE`; (3) the simple accel calibration must be **COMMAND_INT** with `x=4`; (4) SITL's three IMUs disagree until the airframe declares the one it has; (5) `MAV_CMD_SET_GPS_GLOBAL_ORIGIN` does not exist in pymavlink -- the working path is **command 611** as COMMAND_INT; (6) it must be **retried**, because `AP_AHRS::set_origin` only succeeds for the ACTIVE estimator; (7) without an origin the takeoff fails with no statustext; (8) the takeoff altitude is compared against the FCU's GLOBAL altitude; (9) **position setpoints cancel a takeoff** (`guided_set_destination` replaces GUIDED's `SubMode::TakeOff`) |
| 26 | **the sensor was feeding the LIO a 33.3 Hz IMU while every consumer said 200 Hz** | the run's own log said `imu 2538 (33.3 Hz)` and the recorded odometry had `dt` = **29.99 ms**.  Cause: the decimation was derived from a hardcoded 1200 Hz tap while `truth_rx` ran with `--fanout-decimate 6`.  Fixed (decimation MEASURED from the arriving stream, and the noise density uses the same number): sensor log `tap arrives at 200.1 Hz -> one IMU sample every 1`, `imu 13558 (199.4 Hz)`, odometry `dt` 4.998 ms, the FCU's own `VISP`/`VISV` at **48.2 Hz**.  Effect on the mission configuration (3 flights each): **A1 6.40-6.81 -> 4.88-5.08 mm, A2 7.61-8.59 -> 4.70-5.00 mm** |
| 27 | **A3 was measuring my interpolator, not the LIO** | the old A3 resampled both streams onto a 200 Hz grid with `np.interp`; the (since deleted) `tools/a3-offline-ab.py` measured what that does (delivered 2nd difference x2.06, truth low-passed x4.3), and the truth tap is noiseless, so the "ratio to the truth's own jitter" had no pass level at all.  A3 now measures the samples **as delivered** (the FCU never interpolates): delivery rate, wander (rms 2nd difference <= 2.0 mm), outliers (p99 step <= 10 mm), and the worst step is REPORTED with its time rather than gated |
| 28 | **A4: the FCU's own estimate against the truth, and the ~0.09 m vertical datum it exposes** | read from the FCU's own log (`XKF1`), clock aligned by the take-off ramp with the lag **reported**.  Measured (`logs/missionA-imu200a`): horizontal rms **21.37 mm** (<= 50).  The removed datum is a datum: **D -0.0915 m in flight vs -0.1127 m parked**, i.e. the same number before and after take-off, and it is why the truth flies ~0.09 m below the commanded altitude |
| 29 | B1's North bound had 0.06 where East had 0.10 | the commanded rectangle is 2.000 x 1.200 m and B1 was written "<= 2.10 / 1.26"; both axes now carry +0.10, the same tolerance B3 uses, and the legacy `N <= 1.26` bound is still counted and printed beside it |
| 30 | **the flown box was 51 mm South of the intended rectangle -- in the COMMAND, not in the aircraft** | `phase.csv` had it: at every corner the recorded command was `N = 0.348 / 0.350 / 1.550 / 1.548` where the world file's corners are `0.4 / 0.4 / 1.6 / 1.6`.  Cause: the trajectory source projected W->L with the **PREDICTED** calibration while the FSM projected L->F with the **MEASURED** one; the two differ by **56.3 mm**.  Fixed by giving both the same `--calib-file` and printing the W->L->F round trip in `traj.log` (worst **0.000000 mm**); one flight after the fix: command error **E +1.7 / N +2.4 mm**.  Tool: `tools/command-vs-intended.py` |
| 31 | **MISSION A (step b) IS FLOWN: 10 of 10, with the counted table** | `logs/missionA-stepB10-20261013_232331` (10 flights, `ALT_MODE=lio`: `EK3_SRC1_POSZ = VELZ = POSXY = YAW = 6`, `VISO_TYPE 1`, `GPS1_TYPE 0` -- read back from the FCU's own log by `tools/bin-params.py`).  **A1 10 of 10 (4.650-4.900 mm, limit 20); A2 10 of 10 (4.560-4.890, limit 50); A3 continuity 10 of 10; A4 10 of 10 (18.640-21.090, limit 50); B1 10 of 10 (E 2.024-2.060, N 1.247-1.291, limit 2.10/1.30; against the legacy N <= 1.26 it is 4 of 10); B2 39 of 50 legs; B3 40 of 40 corners; B4 10 of 10; completed 10 of 10.**  Evidence `docs/metrics/stepB-rectangle-20261013.txt` |
| 32 | B2's remaining gap is NOT the corner approach -- tested and rejected | the gates were tightened to 40 mm / 30 mm/s and flown 3 times: the corners became tighter (truth arrival **0.033-0.039 m** vs 0.050-0.059) and **B2 got worse: 10 of 15 legs against 39 of 50 (78 %)**.  The shape says why: the worst perpendicular error sits at 16-27 % of the leg, the speed is constant 0.20-0.28 m/s, and the cross-track error swings through zero 2-3 times in 2 m -- a lightly damped lateral response of the position loop at this speed, not an initial condition |
| 33 | the "0.18 m estimator bias" was the BAROMETER, and the arrival test was 3-D | the FSM's 3-D arrival test could never pass while step (a) ran the altitude loop on the baro (`EK3_SRC1_POSZ = 1`); the horizontal error was 3-5 cm all along.  Arrival is now **horizontal** with a documented 0.35 m vertical band, and the first flight with it put every corner within 50-56 mm |
| 34 | B1 was measuring pad-to-square, not the square | taking every truth sample above 0.5 m included the approach from the pad to corner 1, so the box came out 2.54 x 1.57 m for a 2.0 x 1.2 m square.  Windowed to the route it is **2.061 x 1.253 m** against limits 2.10 x 1.26 |
| 35 | the approach speed was the whole of B1/B2 | the inherited `WP_SPD 10.0` / `LOIT_SPEED_MS 12.5` are real-aircraft transit speeds; `params/phase_a_mission.parm` brings the mission to 0.25 m/s with 0.3 m/s^2 limits, after which B2's five legs went from 3 to 5 passing with the worst at 66 mm against 50 mm |

| 37 | **the whole ego-planner line is OUT of the tree, and the pipeline it was built behind is untouched** | 44 files deleted in `1ac78f8` (the patch series `0002..0013`, `planner/ego_adapter.py`, `planner/goal_sequencer.py`, 19 tools, 10 `params/ego_planner_*.yaml`, `rviz/mission_b.rviz`), 2 more in `011b837` (`tools/record-bspline.py`, `tools/world-circle.py`).  `fsm/mission_fsm.py` **1759 -> 1014 lines** and `tools/run-mission.sh` **416 -> 266** in `207f79d`; `grep -i ego fsm/mission_fsm.py` is empty, nothing names the ego workspace, and `planner/` holds one file.  `patches/apm-0001-sitl-truth-tap.patch` and `tools/apm-apply-patch.sh` **stay** |
| 38 | the deletion did not break the recorder chain -- caught by verifying, not by assuming | `tools/record-traj.py` imported `planner/ego_adapter.py` for its pillar clearance, so deleting the adapter would have killed the recorder at start-up and left the flight with no `traj.csv`.  The rule now lives once, in `tools/world-info.py::PillarWorld`, imported by every tool that prints a distance.  Offline check: a point 0.50 m from pillar 0 reads **0.500000 m** through all entry points, and the accepted sortie's own published plan, converted L->W with the run's calibration, has min clearance **0.6000 m** -- the world's designed worst case |
| 39 | **the last flight with no ego node: the square, cleanly** | `logs/missionA-RECT1-noego`, `WORLD=worlds/pillars.yaml`, `MISSION_PARM=phase_a_mission_fast.parm`, `EKF_PARM=ekf_trust_lio.parm`, `ALT_MODE=lio`, `NAV_SOURCE=lio`, `GEOFENCE_M=6.60`.  **A1 4.40 mm, A2 5.96 mm, A4 20.72 mm; B3 all four corners 0.8-7.2 mm; C1 0.022 m; C2 minimum pillar clearance 0.547 m with 0 of 28425 samples inside the 0.20 m footprint [PASS]; B4 landed.**  A3's wander bound FAILed as it was then computed (E 0.527 / N 0.540 / U 2.049 mm against <= 2.00) -- **re-stated in row 45**: the bound is per axis and the axis that decided it was U, but the quantity was being measured over take-off and landing, and over the route window the same stream reads E 0.783 / N 0.833 / U 0.472 mm [PASS] with every other line of the file unchanged; B1 FAILs at E 2.151 m against <= 2.10 (the assertion that the last corner is exactly the first, flown as a 5th leg) |
| 40 | **the corner hold: implemented, flown three times, NOT yet demonstrated** (SUPERSEDED BY ROW 43) | the owner's requirement is a 5 s hover at every corner (`RECT_HOLD_S=5`).  Measured from the truth tap by `tools/dwell-audit.py`: **1 of 4 corners hovered >= 5 s in each of RECT1-noego, RECT2-hold5 and RECT3-hold5** -- and the producer's own log claimed three holds in RECT3 while the track flew only pad -> c2 -> c3.  One defect was found and fixed on the way (`2d60a65`: `densify()` closes the loop it is given, so reusing it per leg made every "leg" an out-and-back c1->c2->c1 -- RECT2's B1 reported an N extent of **0.074 m** over a 7.7 s route, i.e. it never left the first corner, which makes its clean C2 trivial rather than evidence).  RECT3 flew after that fix and still reached only c2 and c3 (B2 legs 2.613 m and 1.196 m long, corners c1 318 mm / c4 1501 mm away).  **SUPERSEDED BY ROW 43 (2026-10-15): the arrival-gated producer plus the tour-flying FSM give 4 of 4 corners hovered >= 5.0 s** |
| 41 | **the cleaned pipeline flies the square: `RECT6-clean-square`, rc=0** | `logs/missionA-RECT6-clean-square`, the same configuration as row 39 with the corner hold explicitly OFF (`RECT_HOLD_S=0`).  **5 leg phases, all five corner visits reached (3.6 / 0.4 / 4.1 / 5.6 / 3.6 mm); A1 4.57 mm, A2 6.42 mm, A4 21.19 mm; B2 4 of 5 legs (leg 4, the second long one, 124.6 mm); C1 0.031 m; C2 minimum pillar clearance 0.552 m with 0 of 28214 samples inside the 0.20 m footprint [PASS]; B4 landed; fsm rc=0, metrics rc=0.**  Beside row 39's numbers (4.40 / 5.96 / 20.72, 0.547 m, 0 of 28425) the two flights are the same flight: same passes, same three failures (B1's East extent 2.155 vs 2.151 m against <= 2.10, B2's leg 4, and A3's wander as then computed -- see row 45), which is what "the cleanup changed nothing measurable" means |
| 42 | **the world file's frozen `hold_s: 3.0` makes the DEFAULT configuration fly one leg, not the square** | `worlds/pillars.yaml` carries `mission.hold_s: 3.0` and is FROZEN; commit `c33e852` made `planner/rectangle_source.py` READ it, so with no `--hold-s` the producer publishes **one leg per message**.  The FSM reads the published path ONCE (`legs()`), so it flies the leg in hand -- **2 corners** -- and lands: MEASURED on `RECT4-clean` (5.0 s dwell, 2 leg phases) and `RECT5-clean` (the world's 3.0 s dwell, 2 leg phases), both rc=0, C2 PASS, B4 landed, and both having visited only c2 and c3 while the producer logged all four legs.  Every flight between `c33e852` and this row (RECT2/RECT3/RECT4/RECT5) is in that mode; RECT1-noego (07:40, before the commit) is not, which is why it is the square.  This is a defect of the corner-hold design, NOT of the 2026-10-15 cleanup -- and it means the tree cannot fly the square in its default configuration until either the FSM re-reads the path per leg or a world with `hold_s: 0` is used.  **FIXED IN ROW 43**: the FSM accepts a leg mid-mission, and the producer now advances ON ARRIVAL instead of on a timer |
| 43 | **THE CORNER HOVER IS FLOWN: 4 of 4 corners hovered >= 5.0 s, measured from the truth tap** | `logs/missionA-RECT7-hold5`, one sortie (`_stage/rect7-hold5-fly.sh`, `RECT_HOLD_S=5.0`, same configuration as row 41), fsm rc=0, metrics rc=0, no ABORT.  `tools/dwell-audit.py <absolute run path> --radius 0.15` prints **"CORNERS HOVERED >= 5.0 s: 4 of 4"**: c1 18.15 / c2 7.67 / c3 7.19 / c4 7.58 s.  The c1 figure is inflated -- the aircraft LANDS on the last corner (C1 0.003 m), so the default run counts its ground time; the new `--airborne-m 0.5` and `--airborne-m 1.5` (both STRICTER, and both also 4 of 4) read 10.01 / 7.93 s for c1 and 7.19-7.67 s for the rest.  Re-judged with the same switch, the four sorties reported as "1 of 4" are **0 of 4** (RECT1, RECT6, RECT2, RECT3), i.e. the hover had never happened at any corner at any altitude, and that "1" was ground time at c1 (RECT3's at c3).  MECHANISM, both halves in the logs: the producer published 4 legs, armed each leg's flight clock when the vehicle was 0.230/0.011/0.004/0.007 m from the leg's start, ARRIVED with the pose 0.062/0.078/0.024/0.079 m from each leg's end (gate 0.100 m), held 5.0 s each (`planner/rectangle_source.py:296-366`); the FSM flew **5 leg phases** (the entry pad->c1 plus the four square legs), held each corner 5.4/6.0/5.7/6.3 s waiting for the next leg (`fsm/mission_fsm.py::_await_next_leg`), and ended the tour on an EMPTY Path.  **The square is really flown**: B2's five legs 0.593 / 2.051 / 1.204 / 2.005 / 1.197 m with worst perpendicular 3.9 / 29.1 / 4.2 / 11.9 / 12.6 mm -- **5 of 5 PASS** (RECT6: 4 of 5, leg 4 at 124.6 mm), the four square legs being the owner's 2.000/1.200/2.000/1.200 m; B1 E 2.154 / N 1.287 m (within the 2.0-2.15 / 1.2-1.3 m the acceptance quotes, and unchanged from RECT1/RECT6's 2.151/2.155, so the dwell cost the box nothing); B3 all five approaches 1.4-2.2 mm; C1 0.003 m; **C2 minimum pillar-surface distance 0.562 m with 0 of 56696 samples inside the 0.20 m footprint [PASS]**; A1 3.42 mm, A2 4.21 mm, A4 17.92 mm, A3 PASS (row 45); B4 landed |
| 44 | **the instrument that lied: `traj.csv`'s `clr_m` is now in the frame its name says** | MEASURED on RECT6 (offline, `tools/traj-clearance-audit.py`, one message of 159): the column read **-0.297153 m** for a plan whose real clearance is **+0.600000 m** -- 0.897 m out -- because the recorder handed the L-frame path to the W-frame pillar rule.  Fixed by applying the same `--calib-file` the FSM and the producer take (`p_W = R_WL @ p_L + t_WL`, yaw +90.0498 deg, t_WL = [-0.00080 -0.00409 +0.11557] m): message 1792023460718192 point 0 goes from (E +0.40366 N -0.50115 U +1.88443) to (E +0.50000 N +0.40000 U +2.00000) = the world file's corner 1 exactly.  AFTER: 0.6000 m on every message, and metrics.txt's own C2 is 0.552 m, so the plan is 0.048 m CLEARER than the flight -- the expected sign (the aircraft can only leave the plan; this run's own B2 measures 124.6 mm on leg 4), within the tool's stated 0.15 m tolerance.  On RECT7 the live recorder's column and the audit's recomputation agree to **0.000000 m** (both 0.6000 m; C2 0.562 m, +0.038 m).  `--calib-file` missing -> `clr_m` is `nan` and the recorder says why.  **C2 is not affected and never was**: it is measured on the flight's own truth inside `tools/metrics.py`.  `tools/plan-vs-truth.py` and `tools/plot-run.py` honour the per-point `frame` column (a fresh recording is W and must not be converted again; an old one is L), and plan-vs-truth prints the old column beside its own recomputation |
| 45 | **A3's wander FAIL adjudicated: the FAIL was real, the QUANTITY was wrong, no bound was changed** | The line read "wander rms 2nd difference E 0.527 / N 0.540 / U 2.049 mm (<= 2.00) [FAIL]" on RECT1 (RECT6: 0.545/0.557/2.077).  From the code: `jit` is per axis and the gate is `np.all(jit <= 2.0)`, so bound and quantity agree -- the quoted 0.5 mm numbers are the EAST axis and it is **U** that decides the gate.  The quantity, though, was measured over EVERY delivered sample, take-off climb and landing descent included (where a 200 Hz second difference is the real vertical acceleration), while `PLAN.md` 3 and `metrics.py`'s own header say ROUTE window.  Recomputed offline on **17 sorties** (the 6 recent ones + the 10 frozen stepB10 flights + the RECT8 regression flight of row 47): whole-stream U 1.784-2.308 mm (the six recent ones 2.049-2.308 = the FAILs, and RECT8's 2.067 would have been the seventh) against route-window U **0.324-0.867 mm on all 17**.  Fixed by measuring the wander over the route window, printing its sample count and time span, and printing the whole-stream value beside it as REPORTED-not-gated; the 2.00 mm per-axis bound is untouched.  Re-stated (written next to each flight's own file as `metrics-restated.txt`): RECT1 A3 PASS E 0.783/N 0.833/U 0.472; RECT6 A3 PASS E 0.807/N 0.857/U 0.491; RECT7 A3 PASS E 0.508/N 0.548/U 0.327 (whole stream 0.419/0.444/1.676); RECT8 A3 PASS E 0.803/N 0.852/U 0.466 (whole stream 0.534/0.553/2.067). `tools/sitl-lio.sh` does not pass it and neither did this pregate, so both start the sensor simulator and die on `FileNotFoundError` while the LIO comes up on an empty feed.  `planner/rectangle_source.py` had the identical default — dead the moment the plan box is run by hand, which `PLANNER=<path.py>` invites. Figure `docs/figures/traj-xy-RECT49-accept-planbox-default.png`.  Gates 17 of 17. |

| 66 | **THE FIRST COMPLETE ego SORTIE ON THIS STACK — and it took three defects, each found only by flying the assembly, and each fixed with the measurement that found it** | **What was assembled**: `PLANNER=ego` with the plan box (`planner/ego_source.py`), the BRIDGE, the SUPERVISOR, `ego_planner_node` and the cloud relay all live at once — the thing that had never run.  **Defect 1, the CLOCK DOMAIN** (`EGO1`): ego's `start_time` is epoch seconds (`ego_replan_fsm.cpp:845`) while the adapter sampled on `time.monotonic()`, so `t = now - start_wall` was about −1.79e9, `bspline_derivs` CLAMPED every sample to the trajectory's START — correct behaviour — and the vehicle was commanded to where it already was for 400 s (`plan_ids=1`, 40241 messages).  **It hid because no offline test crosses two clocks**: `--selftest` drives both sides with ONE synthetic clock, and the parked run passed FOR THE WRONG REASON (its scratch tour put every waypoint within 0.19 m of the pad with `--arrive-m 0.25`, so "the plan's start" was inside the arrival gate). | 67 | **THE OBSTACLE-AVOIDANCE TEST IS BUILT, THE CONTROL PASSES IT, AND EGO **FAILS** IT — the mission had to be defined before it could be run, because no existing tour is obstructed** | **Why a new mission**: every world in the tree threads between pillars with room to spare — MEASURED offline (`_stage/pick-avoidance-mission.py`, minimum clearance to a pillar SURFACE): `forest_all` +0.428 m, `pillars` +0.600, `corridor_fast` +0.700, `forest6x4` +0.962, `pillars_fast` +1.114 — so a planner that ignores obstacles and one that avoids them fly the SAME path and nothing can be demonstrated. | 68 | **THE MAP AUDIT: our relay→map path is FAITHFUL, the pillars on the flown line are ABSENT from ego's map — and the census that would settle why is not yet trustworthy** | **The question DIAG2 left**: ego crossed a 46-pillar field and went through it, while its FSM entered EMERGENCY_STOP six times.  (A) the map was empty there, or (B) the map had the pillars and the planner ignored them.  **Instrumented crossing** (`DIAG4`, recording the map's only input `/ego/cloud` and the map itself `/drone_0_grid/grid_map/occupancy_inflate`, compared against all 46 pillars of `worlds/diagonal.yaml`): **`had a return but NO map cell = 0`** — everything the recorder saw on the relay's output IS represented in ego's occupancy, so **the relay→map path loses nothing and the failure is not in how we feed ego**; and the pillar the aircraft passed **0.23 m** from has **no map cells**, as do the three within 0.6 m of the flown line.  Because ego's map is **STICKY** (outside `camera_pos ± local_update_range` nothing is ever cleared, `plan_env/grid_map.cpp:826-827`, `:234`), a pillar marked even once stays marked for the sortie — so their absence from a map recorded across the whole crossing is evidence they were never marked.  **That is an inference from stickiness, and the headline count is NOT trustworthy**: the cloud recorder subscribes BEST_EFFORT depth 5 and unpacks every point in Python, so it captured 25 792 points where the relay published millions — `32 of 46 pillars never delivered` is therefore contaminated by the RECORDER's loss, and (A) vs (B) is **not settled**.  **A bug of my own, found by the first attempt**: the harness exports `ROS_LOCALHOST_ONLY` but NOT `ROS_DOMAIN_ID` (`tools/run-mission.sh:257`), so it runs on the DEFAULT domain, while the instrumentation exported `ROS_DOMAIN_ID=42` AFTER launching the harness — the recorders were on a different ROS graph from every node they watched, and their 0-byte CSVs looked exactly like an empty topic.  Same class as this session's clock-domain bug: **an unverified assumption about the environment making a working component look like an empty one.**  **WHERE THE ROUND SPENT ITS SORTIES, honestly**: five ego crossings (`DIAG2/3/4/5/6`) all failed C2 with varying severity (min −0.279 / −0.169 / −0.071 / −0.277 / −0.129 m; 13.9 / 9.1 / 4.7 / 18.9 / 11.6 % of samples inside the 0.20 m footprint) — **the failure is real and it varies run to run** — and the last three were spent fighting my own census tooling (`record-cloud.py`'s shallow queue; then `map-census.py` killed mid-spin before printing its verdict; then a double `rclpy.shutdown`).  `_stage/map-census.py` now keeps a running UNION of occupied cells with numpy on the raw buffer, subscribes RELIABLE depth 50, and **prints its verdict every 15 s so a kill cannot lose it** — but it has not yet produced a clean reading, and the next round starts by running it where no watchdog can race it.  Evidence `docs/metrics/diagonal-map-audit-20261015.txt`. |

| 69 | **THE LOSS IS LOCALISED: OUR RELAY IS FAITHFUL, EGO'S MAP IS WHERE THE PILLARS DISAPPEAR — and it loses them in a HALF-SPACE through the vehicle** | **The measurement, parked and race-free** (`_stage/parked-map-census.sh`, evidence `docs/metrics/parked-census-20261015.txt`): SITL + the simulated MID-360 + the LIO + the relay + `ego_planner_node`, vehicle PARKED, three censuses run at once — one per STAGE — each RELIABLE depth 50, each keeping a running UNION of occupied cells with numpy on the raw buffer, each terminating ITSELF after 70 s.  **The counts are healthy enough to trust**: 702 / 181 / 637 messages and 13.7 M / 1.5 M / 10.7 M points observed.  **The result**: `/lio/cloud_world` **34 of 46** pillars → `/ego/cloud` **34 of 46, the same absent list — OUR RELAY LOSES NOTHING** → `/drone_0_grid/grid_map/occupancy_inflate` **23 of 46 — EGO'S MAP DROPS 11 of the 34 it was handed.**  In cells, not just pillars: the input carried **3157** distinct occupied cells, the map kept **1190**, 62 % fewer.  **And the loss has a structure, which is the round's real finding**: the 23 the map kept are essentially every pillar with **x > 0**, the 15 it dropped are essentially every pillar with **x < 0**, and the 12 in neither are scattered and absent from the LIO itself (occlusion from one parked viewpoint — expected, not a defect).  **A HALF-SPACE SPLIT THROUGH THE VEHICLE'S OWN POSITION** points at the map's own acceptance test (`grid_map.cpp:851-855` takes a cloud point only within `local_update_range` OF THE VEHICLE, per axis) rather than at anything random.  **That is a hypothesis and the evidence file says so**; the next round reads the map's own `camera_pos_` against the odometry it is fed.  **Two instrument limits stated rather than buried**: the census is **z-blind** (a 2-D union of x,y, so a per-pillar claim is not a 3-D statement — the three-way comparison is unaffected because all three stages were censused alike), and the periodic verdict **truncates its absent list to 14**, so parsing that read the map as 32 of 46 where the truth is 23 — caught while drawing the figure. | 70 | **I REFUTED MY OWN LAST FINDING: the "half-space split through the vehicle" was an artefact of my census comparing two frames — and with the frame fixed, the cut is real but sits at L_x = −2.5, NOT through the vehicle** | The check took one offline script and no flight.  `tools/map-census.py` compared the occupied cells it sampled (published by EGO'S MAP, which works in **L**) straight against `world["pillars"]` (**W**), without applying the project's own rotation.  **How wrong that is, measured** (`_stage/inspect-field-symmetry.py`): the field is a 10×10 lattice at 0.5 m minus 54 points and it is symmetric under 180° (**44 of 46** pillars map onto a pillar) but **NOT under 90° (0 of 46)** — so an unconverted comparison shares **no** positions with the truth and every "match" was a coincidence.  Last row's counts were not pillar counts and its split was the shape of the coincidence.  **The repair**: the census converts W→L with the same `common/frames.py` the flights use, prints the conversion, and **exits non-zero if the calibration cannot be read** — comparing across frames yields plausible numbers that mean nothing, which is exactly what happened.  **The corrected measurement** (parked, three stages, race-free, 602 / 171 / 548 messages and 11.7 M / 1.4 M / 9.2 M points observed): `/lio/cloud_world` **38 of 46** → `/ego/cloud` **38 of 46, the same absent list — OUR RELAY STILL LOSES NOTHING** → the map **29 of 46**, keeping **1187** distinct occupied cells where its input carried **3169**.  **And a pattern survives, a different one**: every dropped pillar has **L_x ∈ {−2.5, −3.5, −4.5}** while L_y ranges +2.5…−4.5, so it is a **hard cut at L_x = −2.5**, not a y split and **not through the vehicle**, whose own position is the L origin.  The one candidate that makes a hard cut in x is `grid_map.cpp:851-855`, which accepts a cloud point only within `grid_map/local_update_range` OF THE VEHICLE per axis; a 5.5 m half-width cut at −2.5 implies `camera_pos_.x = +3.0`.  **That is a hypothesis with one number in it and this round does not test it** — the next reads what the node actually loaded.  Evidence `docs/metrics/parked-census-20261015.txt`; figure re-rendered from the corrected run. |

| 71 | **THE MAP'S PARAMETERS ARE NOT THE CUT: the node loaded `local_update_range = 5.5 m`, so the acceptance band is ±5.5 and the measured cut at L_x = −2.5 is something else — the round removes its leading candidate, and catches two of my own assumptions doing it** | **Asked of the running node, not read from the launch file** (`_stage/ego-loaded-params.sh`): `grid_map/local_update_range_x/y/z` = **5.5 / 5.5 / 4.5**, `map_size` 16/16/5 m, `resolution` 0.1, `obstacles_inflation` 0.099, `local_map_margin` 10, `ground_height` −0.02, `virtual_ceil_height` 3.0, `pose_type` 0, `use_depth_filter` False, `frame_id` `lio_world`.  **And the wiring is confirmed live**: `/ego/cloud`, `/lio/robo/odom` and `/lio/odom` each have **exactly one subscriber** — the planner node — so no stale publisher, no second consumer, no doubled remap.  **The consequence**: the acceptance band around a vehicle parked at the L origin is x ∈ [−5.5, +5.5], so the band's **own edge is not the cut at −2.5**.  For a 5.5 m band to cut there the map's own position would have to be `camera_pos_.x = +3.0`, and there is no evidence of that — the map reads `grid_map/odom`, remapped to the very stream the acceptance table measures against truth at **3–6 mm** (A1).  **A candidate is removed rather than added**, and it was the only mechanism in that file able to cut in a single axis.  **Two of my own assumptions died in the same run**: `_stage/where-clipped.py` assumed `/tmp/map_census_xy.npy` held the MAP's union — it held the **LIO's** (3265 cells spanning x,y ∈ [−5,+5], smooth in both axes, no cut at −2.5, against the map's 1187), so any conclusion drawn from it would have described the wrong component; and the sign of the offset was printed backwards.  The census now writes **per-topic** files, so the union it reports on is the union it measured.  **Next, and it is one 90 s parked run**: print the x histogram of the MAP's own union — a hard edge names the box that produces it, a smooth one means the loss is per-point rather than per-region, which is a different search.  Evidence `docs/metrics/ego-loaded-params-20261015.txt`. |

| 72 | **THE INSTRUMENT GOES INSIDE THE MAP: it REJECTS NOTHING — and the number that refutes my own census (59 339 cells held, 1187 published)** | Three rounds inferred the map from what it PUBLISHES; the decisive facts are not published at all, and `cloudCallback` silently skips points outside `local_update_range` with no counter.  So **PATCH DIAG/1** was applied inside `plan_env/src/grid_map.cpp` — the project's own method, an in-tree trace with a name like `GOAL-TRACE` — counting points, accepted points and rejections **by axis**, printing the map's own `camera_pos_` and the box it implies, and counting the cells actually marked.  **The map's answer**: `camera_pos=[0.003 0.002 -0.005]`, `range=[5.5 5.5 4.5]`, **`pts=1 909 684, accepted=1 909 684, rejected x/y/z = 0/0/0`** — **not one point is rejected**, so every hypothesis about the acceptance box is dead, including the one this patch was built to test.  **And**: `marked_cells = 59 339` against the **1187** my census found on `/grid_map/occupancy_inflate` — **a factor of fifty**.  **My census was measuring a PUBLISHED SUBSET, not the planner's map**, and the planner never reads that topic: it reads `md_.occupancy_buffer_inflate_` IN PROCESS.  One cause of the subset is visible in the code (`grid_map.cpp:973-975` skips cells above `visualization_truncate_height`, set to 1.8 m by our launch file).  **SO THE LAST THREE ROUNDS' FINDING IS WITHDRAWN**: "ego's map drops the pillars", reported in rows 68–71, is **not established**.  What stands: the relay loses nothing, the map ACCEPTS everything, and the topic the census read is a truncated visualisation.  **Second instrument failure in two rounds** — first two frames compared, now a visualisation measured — and both were found by asking the component itself, so the instrument that works is the one that counts what the consumer actually reads.  The patch took three attempts (the loop's braces appear twice; the first insert used `devi` before its declaration and the compiler said so); it built in 46.4 s, and every attempt restored the pre-patch file first so a failure could not stack.  **Next**: the honest test of the goal's own question is INSIDE the map — ask `getInflateOccupancy` at each of the 46 pillars, the same call the planner makes. | 73 | **THE PLAN GRAZES THE PILLARS: ego's avoidance WORKS and its margin is one voxel — the map was never the problem, and the lever is a number** | **A column that had been recorded all along and never read**: `tools/record-traj.py` writes `clr_m`, the plan's OWN minimum distance to a pillar surface, for every published point.  Read across the diagonal sorties it says the **PLAN ITSELF** contains points **−0.274 / −0.274 / −0.278 / −0.143 m INSIDE a pillar surface** (DIAG2/3/5/6) — so "the plan avoided and tracking lost it" is ruled out, and the failure is the **optimiser's**.  That also finishes closing the map line: the map **accepted all 1 909 684 points** and held **59 339** cells (row 72), and ego's own safety check fired EMERGENCY_STOP six times in DIAG2 — it was finding obstacles.  **And the figure says something sharper than either**: `docs/figures/traj-xy-EGO-plan-through-pillars.png` shows the planned path **curving around** the pillars, taking a wide berth and turning before each one, with red patches only where it **clips a corner**.  **EGO'S AVOIDANCE WORKS AND ITS MARGIN IS TOO SMALL** — the plan's 5th-percentile clearance is −0.02 to +0.20 m while its minimum is −0.27, the signature of a soft collision cost with too little inflation rather than of a blind planner.  **The lever is a number already in our launch file**: `grid_map/obstacles_inflation = 0.099 m` — **ONE voxel** at 0.1 m, and the **only hard margin in that map** (the `optimization/*` distance terms are soft quadratic costs, `bspline_optimizer.cpp:1240,1271`, and the map has no distance query, `grid_map.h:346-366`).  So ego's own definition of "free" is a point **0.10 m** from a pillar, against a C2 row that judges a **0.20 m footprint** in corridors this field measures at about **0.83 m** between faces.  Raising it to ~0.30 m leaves ~0.23 m of free channel — **a hypothesis with arithmetic behind it, to be tested by the next sortie, not asserted here**.  **The map line of investigation is closed**: three rounds (68–72) chased a component that was behaving correctly, and two of those rounds produced findings I had to withdraw.  What finally moved the question was a column recorded all along.  Evidence `docs/metrics/plan-through-pillars-20261015.txt`. |
| 74 | **THE LEVER I PROPOSED LAST ROUND IS REFUTED BY THE SORTIE THAT TESTED IT: `obstacles_inflation` 0.30 m closes this field for ego's A*, and the number in our launch file was never a shortcut of ours -- it is UPSTREAM'S OWN** | Row 73 proposed raising `grid_map/obstacles_inflation` from 0.099 to 0.30 m on arithmetic (a channel of face-to-face gap g is `g - 2*inflation` wide; this field's narrowest gap is 0.814 m, so 0.30 leaves 0.214 m and 0.40 closes it).  The sortie `DIAG7-inflate-030` changed that ONE parameter -- verified in the INSTALLED launch file, not just the repo copy -- and it is unambiguous: **2789** `a star error`, **316 670** `terminal point of the current trajectory is in obstacle`, **191 838** `the drone is in obstacle`, 2399 `Failed to generate direction`, against **0** of each in every 0.099 m crossing.  The vehicle never finished the mission and the watchdog killed it at 420 s (rc=143, hung=1); its plan still contained points 0.262 m inside a pillar.  **Why those counters mean what they say**: `check_collision_and_rebound()` reaches A* only AFTER its hard `getInflateOccupancy` sweep has flagged a trajectory point, and when that A* fails it *drops the segment and keeps the colliding trajectory* (`bspline_optimizer.cpp:1405-1408`) -- so a large count is the map failing its own obstacle test, not a cosmetic warning.  **And the value 0.099 was upstream's**: `advanced_param.launch.py:148` sets `grid_map/obstacles_inflation: 0.099`, i.e. one voxel, deliberately, with the obstacle standoff meant to come from the rebound instead.  Reverted, and the launch file now carries the experiment's result beside the parameter so the next round does not repeat it.  **A hypothesis with arithmetic behind it is still a hypothesis**, and this one cost one sortie to kill. |
| 75 | **THE MAP'S OWN ANSWER, AT LAST MEASURED IN PROCESS: ego's occupancy buffer is a SINGLE-SCAN SHELL -- it held 0 % of the pillar the vehicle was INSIDE while holding 31-39 cells of the pillars a metre away, which is why the planner's own hard collision check found nothing to avoid** | The goal's criterion 3 asks whether ego's map saw the pillars; three earlier rounds inferred it from outside and two of those findings had to be withdrawn, so this one asks the map itself.  **PATCH DIAG/2** dumps `md_.occupancy_buffer_inflate_` -- the array A* and the collision check read, not the truncated visualisation topic -- on a 0.10 m lattice over +-5 m around the vehicle at its own flight height, once a second (63 slices), and counts the rebound's own calls.  **The results, all from `DIAG8-map-slice` (inflation 0.099, rc=0, no hang):** (1) the rebound is ALIVE and was never the problem -- `calls=19864 collided=43 astar_ok=43 astar_fail=0`; (2) the truth spent **2056 of 94124 samples inside a pillar** (2.18 %) in two incidents, #16 (-0.123 m) and #11 (-0.164 m); (3) **the map read OCCUPIED for 34 of those 2056 samples (1.7 %)** and for 0.38 % of all samples; (4) at each incident the slice held **0 % of that pillar's surface** while in the SAME slice pillars #6/#12/#16 at 1.09-1.61 m were held with 31/39/39 cells -- the map was not blind, it was blind to the one obstacle being touched; (5) across all 63 slices, **every** observation with a pillar's centre within 0.50 m found ZERO marked cells (4 of 4), against 24 % zero at 1.0-1.5 m.  **The mechanism is in the source and is structural**: `cloudCallback` calls `resetBuffer(camera_pos +- local_update_range)` (`grid_map.cpp:835`) and then writes `occupancy_buffer_inflate_[idx] = 1` (`:910`) per accepted point -- **a direct write, not an accumulator**.  This variant has NO ESDF, NO ray-casting and NO probability update (`grep` for `updateESDF3D|evaluateEDTWithGrad|distance_buffer_` over the planner tree returns nothing), and `p_hit/p_occ/p_min/p_max` -- all set in our launch file -- are read by nothing on that path.  The map is rebuilt from the CURRENT message alone, one voxel thick, so an obstacle exists in it only while this scan sees it.  **Everything else is exonerated by measurement**: the sensor has a full 360 deg view with 341 points inside 1 m; the map receives ~24 000 points/s and **rejects none** (0/0/0 of 1.5 M); our relay kept 35.0 % at exactly the map's own 0.10 m cell size and cropped 1.9 %.  **It also explains the optimiser**: `calcDistanceCostRebound` measures distance to `cps_.base_point`/`direction`, which in this SWARM variant are OTHER DRONES' points -- empty for one vehicle -- so `lambda_collision` and `dist0` multiply nothing.  **Not established**: WHY a pillar goes unmarked below ~0.5 m when the cloud still carries near points (three candidates, listed in the evidence).  The fix does not need that answer: if the map cannot remember, the RELAY can.  Evidence `docs/metrics/diagonal-map-truth-20261016.txt`, figure `docs/figures/round8/map-representation-vs-distance.png`. |

It is valid because the cloud is in the WORLD frame, where a static world's points do not move: the union of consecutive scans is the swept view, not a smear -- and it is keyed on the same 0.10 m lattice the map stores, so nothing is invented at a finer resolution than ego can hold.  The value comes from the geometry rather than taste: a pillar unmarked at 0.4 m was marked at 1.0-1.5 m a second or two earlier, and the measured closing speed on this mission is ~0.4 m/s, so 3 s spans the whole approach the map forgets.  **The A/B is one flag on one mission** (`worlds/diagonal.yaml`, `PLANNER=ego`, identical parameters): the truth's closest approach to a pillar SURFACE goes from **-0.164 m to +0.150 m**, samples inside a pillar from **2056 of 94124 to 0 of 100120**, and the C2 row's own count from **5356 of 48943 to 2108 of 53753** -- with rc=0, no hang, C1 PASS at 0.003 m and A1/A2 unmoved (6.25 mm / 3.08 mm, so this is not a tracking effect).  **A swing of +0.314 m in the only number that describes whether obstacles were avoided.**  **And the same instrument says why, not just that**: PATCH DIAG/2's map slice read on both runs shows the map holding 31-40 cells of a pillar at 0.5-1.5 m where it held 3-28, and 14-24 cells at 2-5 m where it held 0-18 -- the union also defeats occlusion, since an obstacle seen from an earlier viewpoint survives the moment the current scan loses it.  **Not claimed**: C2 still FAILS (2108 samples sit between +0.150 and +0.200 m, so the vehicle clears the pillars but not by the 0.20 m the row asks for); the 0.42 m band is still imperfect; and one A/B is not a distribution -- every earlier crossing failed C2 with wide scatter (-0.071 to -0.279 m), so the window reproduces before it becomes a default, which is exactly why `EGO_CLOUD_WINDOW` defaults to 0.0. | 78 | **CRITERION 4 IS WITNESSED: the ego plan box is SIGKILLed mid-route and the supervisor aborts BY NAME, lands, and disarms -- and the rule that keeps SILENCE and COMPLETION apart is shown in BOTH directions on the same stack** | The bench is `_stage/f2-kill-bench.sh`'s (`RECT25-kill-plan`) method moved to this planner (`_stage/ego-kill-plan.sh`): fly the diagonal, wait for the supervisor to say the plan box is talking, wait 8 s, kill it.  **Nothing is killed by name** -- the PID is the unique `pgrep -f 'planner/ego_source.py'` match, the matched command line is printed to the log first, and the count must be exactly 1 or the script refuses.  **The supervisor's own words**: `ABORT: THE PLAN BOX HAS STOPPED TALKING: nothing on /plan/cmd for 3.0 s (limit 3.0 s) after 534 messages, trajectory_id 1 -- a plan box that is DONE says so in the message (trajectory_flag = COMPLETED); SILENCE IS NOT COMPLETION`, then `landed: armed=False mode=LAND` and `phase -> done`.  `phase.csv` reads **`preflight takeoff hold leg1 abort land done`** -- and that `abort` phase is exactly the thing the pre-F2 code could not produce, because a dead plan box and a finished tour used to be the same fact.  **And the other direction, without which the first proves nothing**: on the two runs that COMPLETED the tour (`DIAG9`, `DIAG10`) there is no abort at all and the bridge's shutdown line reads **`plan_age = 0.00 s`** -- still publishing at the end -- against **8.20 s** here. The DIAG/2 slices there are **10201 of 10201 cells '?' -- the whole window outside the map** -- because the map's z origin IS `grid_map/ground_height = -0.02`, so `isInMap` fails, `getInflateOccupancy` returns -1, and `check_collision_and_rebound` reads -1 as OCCUPIED (`bspline_optimizer.cpp:1329`).  Hence **1 058 710** `the drone is in obstacle. This should not happen.` and an FSM that never planned again: `GEN_NEW_TRAJ` **19 049** against `EXEC_TRAJ` **207**.  **A landed vehicle is outside its own map, and its own position then reads as an obstacle.**  **Withdrawn**: 'two cells collapses the search', 'two cells is worse than three because the window thickens the map', and 'there is no value between 1 and 2 cells, so the map cannot supply the C2 footprint' -- one cell is measured to work and two is measured not to, but the reason is a z-boundary deadlock, so **the inflation budget is untested above one cell rather than exhausted**.  **Not explained**: why the vehicle descended at all -- it flew 1.8 m to within 0.10 m of a pillar surface and lost its trajectory, and whether the two-cell map made the first plan go there or the adapter brought it down is not measured.  **And a standing defect for the whole stack**: any ego sortie in which the vehicle touches down with the planner running can deadlock this way. ## Next, in order

1. **B1 IS THE ONLY ROW STILL FAILING, and its mechanism is now settled end to end.** The FCU's position
   loop rings when a leg brakes into a corner (pass at 0–5 mm, 99–142 mm inside, 35–93 mm past, both
   extremes at ~0 m/s), the 5 s dwell is shorter than the ~4.3 s ring, and the box records the envelope.
   Approach shaping was BUILT AND FLOWN (`8ce59c3`): E growth +109.6 mm, N +69.0 mm, N PASSES, E misses by
   **9.6 mm**, at no measurable time cost — and the reason it cannot cross is measured (the plan advances
   from the shaping point while the aircraft is still swinging, so the ring is MOVED, not damped).  The two
   remaining levers, with their costs, are in `docs/metrics/b1-approach-shaping-20261016.txt`: a tighter
   arrival gate on the shaped waypoints (the ring's own ~4.4 s decay at four waypoints), or damping the loop
   / the slow arm, which already PASSES at +38.4/+55.0 mm.  **THE BOUND IS 2.10 / 1.30 AND DOES NOT MOVE.**
2. **The velocity staircase itself is NOT fixed** — only the parameter that describes it: `/lio/odom` is
   still 10.01 Hz of information held into a ~47 Hz stream.  An honest rate fix means either the LIO's
   publication or the bridge's relay rule carrying the sample's AGE, so a consumer can discount a repeat;
   the A/B that would show whether it matters is already instrumented (A4-v).
3. **A4-v IS A NEW ROW WITH NO HISTORY**: 0.069 m/s rests on one arm plus three reproducing sorties against
   a five-sortie control band.  A longer record would make it a baseline rather than a result.
4. **`RECT36-ratio`'s c2 dwell (4.14 s) is the single miss in 72 corner visits**, and its shape matches
   RECT11's (row 49: one arrival split in two by the ring).  The tool that names the split is
   `tools/corner-dwell-trace.py <run> <corner>`; the lever is the same ring B1 measures, so it belongs with
   item 1 rather than beside it.
5. **THE LOCKED BASELINE'S PROVENANCE IS NOW EXPLICIT**: the three accepted sorties (RECT23/24/26) were
   flown on `params/ekf_trust_lio.parm`, and the promoted configuration is a SEPARATE, named file with its
   own four sorties.  Any future acceptance claim must say which of the two it was flown on — the manifest
   carries both, and `tools/run-mission.sh` prints the override and this repo's own HEAD at start-up.
6. **THE EGO SORTIE'S ASSEMBLY IS DONE; what remains is its AVOIDANCE MARGIN, and that is now a named,
   measured defect rather than a mystery.**  Since this item was written, `PLANNER=ego` has flown the full
   mission with the BRIDGE and the SUPERVISOR attached (row EGO4: five waypoints, `trajectory_id` 1->5,
   sustained COMPLETED, no gate abort), and the diagonal crossing has been flown eleven times.  Rows 73-75
   close the diagnosis: the plan grazes because **the map the planner plans against is a single-scan shell**
   that holds nothing of an obstacle within ~0.5 m, so a trajectory through a pillar's middle is not a
   collision by ego's own test -- and that is why `check_collision_and_rebound` logged ZERO failures on the
   very crossings that C2 caught.  **The next experiment is in OUR code, not ego's**: make
   `src/planner/cloud_relay.py` forward a short sliding-window UNION of recent scans instead of one scan, so
   the shell gains the memory the planner assumes it has -- then re-fly the diagonal and read the SAME
   DIAG/2 slice and the same C2 row.  Two other pieces of the planner goal are still unwitnessed and are
   cheaper than they look: the ABORT path (criterion 4: stall the plan box mid-route and confirm a
   named-reason abort plus a landing without breaking the 'keeps talking after COMPLETED' rule), and the
   RECTANGLE-vs-EGO comparison on one mission, which needs no new flight -- DIAG1 and DIAG8 are the same
   mission and differ in exactly the planner.

## Open questions, and the things kept for a decision

1. **`--follow path` and `--stall-topic` in `fsm/mission_fsm.py`**: both were kept deliberately.
   `--follow path` is a generic look-ahead consumer of the same frozen Path (the rectangle does not need
   it); `--stall-topic` turns "the trajectory source has stopped producing plans" into a NAMED abort, and
   the dropped planner chain was its only publisher, so today nothing raises it.  Neither is ego code --
   they are interfaces a future source may want -- but neither has a user today.
2. **`docs/metrics/ego-*.txt` and `phaseB-*.txt` stay.**  They are the written record of the dropped line,
   they are not edited, and nothing in the pipeline reads them.  The figures under
   `docs/figures/archive/mission_b/` (moved there on 2026-10-16 when the owner asked for `docs/figures/` to be
   tidied -- MOVED, not deleted: the record is intact and the paths in these old files are stale by one
   directory) and the world `worlds/forest_b.yaml` stay for the same reason.  Two of them
   name `tools/scan_relay.py`, which row 46 deleted: they are the record of what existed then, and editing
   them would falsify it.
3. **`rviz/mission_a.rviz`'s comment mentions the deleted `rviz/mission_b.rviz`.**  It is one of the 49
   frozen artifacts, so `tools/frozen-check.py` refuses to let it be edited; the stale cross-reference is
   recorded here instead.
4. Should the simulator still carry a compass (never an EKF source) so heading can be cross-checked?
5. The old `gazebo_apm` tree holds ~40 historical documents (the evidence chain for rounds 1-7): fold it
   into the documentation budget, or leave it frozen?

A tool therefore has TWO roots: the code root (`<repo>/src`, for `sys.path`) and the project root (`<repo>`, for `worlds/`, `params/`, `docs/`, `frozen/`).  `python3 src/tools/test-layout.py` is the gate for that distinction and runs with the other offline tests. * **Before every commit**: `python3 tools/frozen-check.py` (must print `50 of 50 files unchanged`) and

* `_stage/` is scratch space OUTSIDE the repo (flight scripts, calibrations, one-off probes).  The

## Mission B (ego-planner): dropped — what it taught us

Dropped by the owner 2026-10-15; deleted from the tree the same day (`1ac78f8`, `011b837`).  What survives
and was worth the line: the frozen trajectory interface (`PLAN.md` §4.1) -- a planner arrived behind it and
left again, and not one line of the FSM changed.

* **Interface gates we ADDED, then DELETED** (`planner/ego_adapter.py`): an arena + 0.20 m footprint gate on
  every published path, a WITHHOLD-with-a-reason for an expired or degenerate spline (`9d0e744`), the
  re-projection of a plan onto the vehicle, `/planner/stalled`.  DELETED: the "start within 0.50 m of the
  vehicle" gate (`19766bc`) -- the refusal, not the far start, starved three sorties.
* **The anchoring defect**: `EGOPlannerManager::reboundReplan`'s REPLAN branch resamples
  `local_data_.position_traj_` from `t_cur` and never reads `start_pt`, so `spline(0) = old_traj(t_cur)` --
  where the OLD PLAN believes the vehicle is.  Anchored by `0013`/`7412be3`;
  `docs/metrics/phaseB-AP8-anchor-20261015.txt`.
* **The clock defect**: `EGOReplanFSM::execFSMCallback` tests arrival on the TRAJECTORY's clock
  (`t_cur > duration - 1e-2` -> WAIT_TARGET), so a vehicle slower than `manager/max_vel` makes the planner
  declare arrival and stop planning (`0010`/`475342f`; `docs/metrics/ego-offline-lag-ab.txt`).
* **The crash defects**: `0002` -- a goal before the first odometry segfaults, a `spin_some` in a callback
  aborts the node, the goal's altitude is discarded; `std::bad_alloc` killed the node 35 s into AP5 with
  nothing watching the children; the stall escalation died of `NameError: name 'Bool' is not defined`
  until `7a00430`.
* **The pool-exhaustion finding**: `[ERROR] [Coord2Index]: Ran out of pool, index=88 100 25,
  POOL_SIZE=100 100 100` with 10468 x `AstarSearch: Unable to handle the initial or end point` and 10167 x
  `check_collision_and_rebound: ERROR! the drone is in obstacle` -- the map pool is exhausted, so the A* and
  the ESDF that should do the avoiding error instead and the FSM cycles EMERGENCY_STOP <-> GEN_NEW_TRAJ
  (`docs/metrics/phaseB-AP8-anchor-20261015.txt`).
* **The result**: no sortie met both halves of the acceptance (0 of N inside the 0.20 m footprint AND
  >= 10 of 12 stops reached) -- AF1 had 8 of 12 stops and 142220 of 305081 samples (46.62 %) inside the
  footprint; AO2/AG1/AP1/AP3 held C2 with 1 of 12 stops.  Corner dwell from the tap
  (`tools/dwell-audit.py`): **0 of 4** hovered >= 5 s in each of the last three ego sorties
  (`-AP6-rect-dyn`, `-AP7-rect-fixed`, `-AP8-anchor`), **1 of 4** in each of the three no-ego sorties that
  followed (`-RECT1-noego`, `-RECT2-hold5`, `-RECT3-hold5`), all under `logs/missionA-`.

The other **28 top-level figures moved to `docs/figures/archive/`** with `git mv`, so the history and the blobs survive; nothing was deleted, because 26 of them are cited by name in `docs/metrics` and STATUS, and a citation whose file exists under `archive/` is recoverable while a deleted one is not** | The rule being applied is criterion 5's own: the top level holds top views, and the owner asked for the latest one.  **What the cleanup taught**: the frozen set is pinned by PATH, so any future reorganisation of `docs/figures/` has to consult `frozen/mission_a.manifest` first -- and the check that caught it is exactly the tool that exists for this (it printed `MISSING docs/figures/traj-xy-RECT46-accept-promoted.png` and refused until it was restored).  **Where the historical figures are now**: `docs/figures/archive/<name>.png` for the 28 moved plus the 62 that were already there (91 files); the analysis figures that were never top views stay in their round directories (`round7/`, `round8/`, `round15/`, `round70/`, `round71/`, `round75/`, `round76/`, `mission_a/`). | 141 | **THE EGO ARM'S ACCEPTANCE ROW IS RESTATED IN THE ONLY FRAME THE PLANNER USES (owner's decision, no flight): 0.20 m becomes C2-ego = ">= 95 % of truth samples at >= 0.10 m AND 0 samples inside a pillar body", because 0.10 m is ONE MAP CELL and at this field's tightest pair that cell IS the free channel -- and because 0 of 32 existing ego crossings can meet 0.20 m while the restated row discriminates (23 of 32 for the fraction, 10 of 32 for the body count)** | The question was the owner's: *"0.8 m is too hard to satisfy -- is there a way to make the narrowest passage about 0.6 m?"*  **The arithmetic, all of it read from files rather than remembered**: the tightest 68 pillar pairs in `worlds/diagonal.yaml` sit **1.414 m** between centres (r = 0.30 m), i.e. **0.814 m face to face**; `grid_map/obstacles_inflation` 0.099 m at `resolution` 0.1 m gives `inf_step = ceil(0.099/0.1) = 1` (`plan_env/src/grid_map.cpp:653`) and `cloudCallback` **hard-codes `inf_step_z = 1`** (`:841`), so the inflation is a **27-CELL CUBE** around every marked voxel -- +0.10 m on the axes, but **+0.1*sqrt(2) = 0.141 m at the in-plane corner**.  The free channel the planner actually works with is therefore **0.616 m** on an axis-aligned corridor and **0.532 m** at 45 deg, and the owner's 0.6 m is not a relaxation of the task: it is the width the task already has.  **What follows is the part that makes the row honest**: a standoff of 0.10 m from a pillar surface IS one map cell IS the edge of that channel, so "keep >= 0.10 m" and "stay inside the region your own map calls free" are the same sentence -- the new row is the channel's own definition, not a number picked to pass.  **Instrument**: `_stage/c2-prime.py`, truth from `tap.csv`, plan points from `traj.csv.points.csv`, and the per-plan `clr_m` column rows 73/79/88 quote from `traj.csv`; what cannot meet it is **this planner** (0 of 32, mean -0.101 m, sd 0.143 m) for the reasons rows 73-88 established -- every A* step costs a constant so the search returns the shortest path through the corridor (`dyn_a_star.cpp:217`), this swarm variant has **no obstacle-distance cost** (`calcDistanceCostRebound` measures against other drones' points, empty for one vehicle), and the map is a single-scan shell that often does not mark a pillar nearer than ~0.50 m; (c) the map-frame form of the same sentence ("the truth never enters a cell ego's map marks OCCUPIED") is deliberately **a DIAGNOSTIC, not the bar** -- an unmarked pillar is a free cell, and a bar a blind map can pass is exactly what three earlier instrument corrections were about.  **The 0.20 m row is UNCHANGED for the stack's own arm** (rectangle/FSM: 0.562 m, 0 of 56 696 samples inside). the FSM never reacted because `:1201`/`:1213` evaluate the PLAN and `odom_pos_` is never tested against the map anywhere in the FSM (`Suddenly discovered obstacles` 0, `current traj in collision` 0, `Depth Lost` 0); Evidence `docs/metrics/why-collide-20261016.txt`; two of my own conclusions were corrected by the audits (a lag that is really cross-track drift; and the setpoint frame error already retracted in row 149). |

| 156 | **THE HEADING: ego DOES compute "toward the next waypoint" -- AND THE BRIDGE WAS THROWING IT AWAY ON EVERY SETPOINT.  `traj_server::calculate_yaw` takes the direction to a point `time_forward_` (1.0 s) ahead along the spline, limits it to pi rad/s and low-passes it; `bridge_node.py:193` did `yaw = s.yaw if self.yaw_rad is None else self.yaw_rad` with `--yaw` DEFAULTING TO 0.0, so the relay replaced it on all 1664 rows of the last flight (`wire.csv`: `yaw_l` swept +-3.14 rad, `yaw_out` = 0.000000 every row). So the answer to "xva+yaw?" is: **x + v + yaw today; the a field exists in the message and is discarded by the mask.**  **The fix, minimal and per-arm**: `--yaw` default None = pass the plan's heading through; `run-mission.sh` now passes `--yaw 0.0` for `PLANNER=rectangle` only, so mission A is byte-identical to what was flown and accepted (`planner/cmd_stream.py` records that `--yaw-mode fixed` / `--yaw 0.0` is what every mission-A sortie streamed), while the ego arm follows its path.  **Consequence worth recording**: the heading also pins the pointing of the 45-degree-down lidar, so this changes what the map sees while turning -- an effect the next flight's map-slice comparison can measure.  Gates after the change: `run-mission.sh` syntax, planner-knob, planner-swap and lint all pass.  **Not yet flown**: the heading change goes into the air with the next sortie, together with the owner's "keep v small" arm (`EGO_MAX_VEL`/`EGO_MAX_ACC`, which feed all four `optimization/`+`manager/` ceilings and the two `bspline/limit_*` feasibility terms) and the results of the patch-cleanup audit that is running now. |

EFFECT: eight arrivals out of eight in Flights I and J, each logged at 0.325-0.349 m horizontal, closing at 0.41-2.92 m/s.  **(2) 9-10 s OF STANDSTILL PER CORNER** (`_stage/leg-budget.py`): `RECT_HOLD_S` 5.0 -> 0.3 s and the settle gate 0.08 -> 0.  **(3) THE PLAN'S OWN SPEED CAP**: `EGO_MAX_VEL` 0.8 -> 3.0 -> 2.5, `EGO_MAX_ACC` likewise; MEASURED on the reference itself, Flight E p90 0.770 / max 0.991 m/s -> Flight H p90 2.716 / p99 3.068 / max 3.334 m/s.  **(4) THE REFERENCE IS NOW CONTINUOUS**: `t_cur = now - start_time` restarts at every 1 Hz re-plan so the forwarded point STEPS (Flight A: median 0.281, max 0.927 m); `--ref-clamp-m` bounds how FAR (0.35 -> 0.80 -> 1.20 m as the speed rose -- in Flight H the 0.80 was BINDING at a measured max |cmd-fcu| of 0.840) and the NEW `--ref-slew-mps 6.0` bounds how FAST, turning each step into a ramp.  **(5) AND THE ONE THAT WAS THE MISSION: THE MAP WAS 0.420 m FATTER THAN THE WORLD.**  `_stage/map-inflation.py` on each flight's OWN occupancy slices, outer marked cell from a pillar's axis (true surface 0.300 m): Flight E 0.540 (inflation 0.099), Flight I **0.720** (inflation 0.0, relay voxel 0.10, relay window 3.0 s), Flight J **0.380** (inflation 0.0, relay voxel 0.03, relay window 0.0 s). The obvious mechanism, and the one to test first, is the MAVLink budget on the ONE `tcp:127.0.0.1:5760` link the bridge owns: at `--nav-hz 48` plus `--wire-hz 100` that is 148 packets a second on an endpoint that has carried 98 (48 + 50) in every sortie so far, and `bridge_node.py`'s own docstring says "the wire rate stays 50 Hz, because 50 Hz is what every mission-A sortie flew". | 178 | **THE 100 Hz WIRE IS NOT AVAILABLE ON THIS STACK, AND THE TWO SORTIES SEPARATED WHY: Flight AA's own bridge counters read `plan_rx=3028  sent=1288  refused=0  nav_hz=47.0  plan_age=9.86` -- the bridge RECEIVED the plan messages and SENT the setpoints with ZERO refusals, so MAVLink was never the limit; what died was the ROS side, `plan_age` 9.86 s meaning `traj_server` had published nothing for ten seconds when the sortie was stopped. So the 0.04 m the 50 Hz staircase costs cannot be bought back by turning the knob: it needs the bridge made cheap (a C++ publisher, or the setpoint stream moved off the Python relay path), or the sample-and-hold removed at its source by publishing the reference at the rate `traj_server` already produces it.  **WHAT STANDS, at the end of this goal's rounds**: the ego replacement is DONE and verified (8 ROS 2 packages, colcon rc=0, 74 parameters, 0 undeclared; `p_hit`/`p_miss` raycast live; PATCH RESOLUTION/1 and PATCH I re-applied and each proved by a run's own output); the LIDAR MAP IS THE RIGHT MAP (reference clear in 4 of 4 and 5 of 5 contact episodes across Flights X and Z, where the pre-swap flights had it INSIDE); the best measured arm on this tree is **Flight Z** (lidar map 0.1 m, PATCH I: **5 of 8 legs under 6.0 s**, A1 **9.19 mm**, C1 **0.072 m**, B1 8.599/8.384, tracking gap p50 0.236); and the ONE remaining obstacle is the tracking tail -- p90 **0.469 m** against the **0.200 m** the route's geometry leaves -- which is controller-side and was measured unchanged by the swap.  The two levers that could move it are now both REFUTED with evidence (the 0.05 m lattice in Flight Y, the 100 Hz wire in Flights AA/AB), and the next one is structural: make the bridge cheap enough to publish at 100 Hz, or bind the mission's line in the shell with a corridor clamp, or give the route more room (there is none in this field). |

This is the `posvelacc` experiment, and every reason it was refuted before is now GONE: Flight D's only trial was invalidated when its LIO was found to have diverged 3.65 m, and the pre-condition the review set for it -- make the reference continuous first -- has since been met (`--ref-clamp-m` 1.20 and `--ref-slew-mps` 6.0), so the acceleration now riding the wire would be smooth.  **(2) the position-loop gain** `PSC_NE_POS_P` 1.0 (never varied here): a proportional position loop with a velocity feed-forward leaves a cross-track error proportional to the reference's lateral acceleration, and 1.0 s^-1 is soft.  **(3) the heading**, which the bridge now passes through from the plan: the tilt direction follows the yaw, so a yaw that lags the turn turns a forward-acceleration demand into a lateral one.  NEXT: fly `BRIDGE_FIELDS=posvelacc` with the acceleration CLAMPED in the contract shell (the clamp that Flight D lacked -- its spike rode the unclamped acceleration field), then `PSC_NE_POS_P` at 1.5 if the mask does not close the gap. AL's own cost is time: legs 2.22 / 6.03 / 7.81 / 6.02 / 7.27 / 6.54 / 8.46 / 7.13 s (1 of 8 under 6 s, max 8.46), because |v_ref| p50 1.100 m/s is the corner governor's cap -- the plan is slow, not the clamp. |

The aircraft then needed **1.6 s** to come back to the line, which is the 1.5 s^-1 position loop's own decay. | **THE GEOMETRY THAT KILLS THE OPENING**: on this route the pillar rows are 0.5 m from EVERY edge -- the corners are not an exception, because the nearest pillar to a corner is 0.707 m from it on the diagonal, so a reference that swings 0.45 m off the line near a corner is swinging toward a row, not into free space.  The +0.407 m at the corners is the clearance of the CORNER POINT itself, not a licence to leave the line.  `EGO_CORRIDOR_OPEN_M` therefore defaults to **0.0** (with `EGO_CORRIDOR_END_M` 0.0) and Flight AM flies the corridor at the same width at the corners as in the middle -- the pre-registered prediction is that the reference's clearance min stays >= 0.100 m while the aircraft's inside-body fraction falls below AL's 0.79 %, because what is left is only the aircraft's own decay after a turn, not a lead it was invited to take. |

| 192 | **THE CORNER SPEED IS THE SAFETY KNOB AND THE PLAN'S SPEED IS NOT THE TIME KNOB: Flight AP (corridor 0.01 m + `EGO_CORNER_MPS` 1.10 -> **0.80**) is the safest sortie ever measured -- **C2 minimum -0.031 m** (AN -0.052, AD -0.134, AL -0.271, AK -0.276), **0.17 %** of samples inside a body (AN 0.21, AM 0.66, AD 0.34), **0.37 %** below the 0.10 m standoff (AN 0.99, AK 13.97), `truth clearance` minimum in the instrument window **+0.078 m with 0 of 2581 samples inside a body**, A1 9.35 mm, A2 7.29 mm, **B1 E 6.378 / N 8.210** (the closest to 6.10 / 8.10 of any sortie), C1 0.069 m -- while Flight AO (the same corridor with `EGO_MAX_VEL`/`ACC` 2.0 -> **2.5**, i.e. asking the PLAN for speed) goes the other way on both axes: inside 0.74 %, minimum -0.054 m, ACROSS-the-plan p50 0.204 / p90 0.335 m (AN 0.124 / 0.276), and the legs do not improve enough to matter (3 of 8 under 6 s, **max 10.66** against AP's 3 of 8 and 9.89). That is why 'raise max_vel' cannot reach the 6 s criterion: the plan is slow only where the governor is holding it.  The position slew limiter is already TAPERED across `corner_m`, so only the hard velocity cap makes the whole radius slow.  Flight AQ therefore shrinks the radius (`EGO_CORNER_M` 1.0 -> **0.5**) while keeping AP's 0.80 m/s, so the deep cap applies only where the vertex momentum is actually generated, and Flight AR then adds the speed back on top. |
| 193 | **FLIGHT AR IS THE FIRST SORTIE IN THIS WHOLE INVESTIGATION THAT NEVER ENTERS A PILLAR: C2 minimum **+0.032 m** (positive -- the 1200 Hz truth never comes closer to a pillar surface than 3.2 cm), **0.00 %** of samples inside a body, 0.86 % below the 0.10 m standoff, so **C2-ego is MET** (>= 95 % at >= 0.10 m AND 0 inside a body) for the first time on this route -- together with A1 10.33 mm, A2 7.87 mm, C1 0.061 m [PASS], B1 E 6.451 / N 8.271, and the leg times **0.88 / 5.44 / 6.27 / 4.86 / 6.40 / 4.84 / 6.61 / 4.30 s -- 5 of 8 under 6.0 s, max 6.61 s** -- i.e. ONE criterion is now met and the other is 0.27-0.61 s away on the three 8 m edges.
