| 199 | **A BARE `PLANNER=ego` SORTIE NOW FLIES THE NEW ARM AND READS IT BACK: Flight AY was run with NO environment variable at all (only `EXTRA_PARM`, the FCU's limit file), so it is what a reader of the docs gets: **C2 minimum -0.003 m** (three millimetres), **0.06 %** of samples inside a body, 1.00 % below the 0.10 m standoff, A1 10.32 mm, A2 10.68 mm, B1 E 6.295 / N 8.392, C1 0.140 m [PASS], route 38.0 s, and legs **1.00 / 4.71 / 5.59 / 4.50 / 6.77 / 5.73 / 6.19 / 4.55 s -- 6 of 8 under 6.0 s, max 6.77 s** -- with the reference never inside a pillar (0.00 %) and its worst clearance +0.276 m.  Together with AR (+0.032) and AT (+0.029) that is THREE sorties of this arm inside 3.5 cm of zero, against Flight AK's -0.276 m / 7.42 % for the same harness before the corridor, and against Flight AD's -0.134 / 0.34 % which was the best the project had before today. | **THE DAY, IN FOUR NUMBERS** (all `PLANNER=ego` on `worlds/rect68.yaml`, 46 pillars, 6 x 8 m, two laps): samples inside a pillar body **7.42 % -> 0.06 %**; minimum pillar-surface distance **-0.276 m -> -0.003 m** (and +0.032 m on the best run); the plan's own reference inside a pillar **10.18 % -> 0.00 %**; legs under 6 s **1 of 8 -> 6 of 8** at the default and **8 of 8** with `EGO_MAX_VEL=3.0`; route duration **73.7/47.8 s -> 38.0 s (default) / 31.2 s (AU)**.  The mechanism is one knob and one measurement: the plan does not hold a line it was never given, the world's centrelines are the maximum-clearance paths (+0.200 m exactly), and holding the reference on them converts the error budget from lateral (which spends clearance) to along-track (which spends none).  **WHAT IS STILL OPEN, stated so it cannot be misread**: the objective asks for BOTH clauses in ONE sortie; the clean clause is met at 2.5 m/s and the time clause at 3.0 m/s, the two are 0.5 m/s apart on `EGO_MAX_VEL`, and the residual run-to-run spread at a fixed arm is ~0.04 m, i.e. the same order as the 3 mm by which the default still grazes.  Closing that last 3 mm needs either a faster controller (the tracking gap's p50 is 0.14-0.19 m at 1.5 m/s and the pillar rows leave 0.200 m) or a route with more room -- there is none in this field. |
| 200 | **THE DEFAULT ARM'S DISTRIBUTION OVER FIVE SORTIES, AND A CORRECTION: “clean” IS 0 +- 0.03 m, NOT 0 +0.03 m.  AR +0.032 / AT +0.029 / AY **-0.003** / AY2 **-0.033** / AZ **-0.238** m of minimum clearance, with 0.00 / 0.00 / 0.06 / 0.17 / 0.31 % of samples inside a body -- the same arm (corridor 0.01 m, corner 0.50 m at 1.00 m/s, ceiling 2.5, everything else Flight AD's), and the REFERENCE on the centreline in every one of them (0.00 % inside a body, worst-instant reference clearance +0.269 to +0.276 m).  So the reference's clearance has become a property of the world while the AIRCRAFT's has not, and the arm sits ON the surface rather than clear of it. | **WHAT THE BAD RUN IS** (Flight AZ, and it is the same signature as Flight AK's before the corridor): the plan was SLOW and erratic -- `|v_ref|` p50 **0.922** m/s, route **53.1 s**, legs 8.64 / 8.22 / 8.39 / 6.78 / **12.46** / 4.19 s -- and the truth's worst sample is **0.548 m** off the route at (-2.46,-4.55), i.e. 0.55 m OUTSIDE the south edge into the y = -4.5 pillar row, 0.04 m from the (-2.5,-4.5) pillar's axis, with the reference 0.010 m off the line.  A slow plan is not a gentle one: ego replanning through a map it is unhappy with produces a jerky reference and the aircraft follows it badly, which is why the two worst sorties of the day (AK -0.276, AZ -0.238) are the two slowest (73.7 s and 53.1 s) and the two best (AU/AY 31-38 s) are among the fastest.  **THE HONEST SUMMARY OF THE STATE** is therefore: the corridor fixed the PLAN-side defect completely (10.18 % of reference samples inside a pillar -> 0.00 %), it took the aircraft-side collision rate from 7.42 % to 0.00-0.17 % on 4 of 5 runs, the time criterion is met at 3.0 m/s (AU: 8 of 8 legs at 3.55-5.30 s), and the last two things that stand between the sortie and the owner's objective are (a) the aircraft's 0.15-0.35 m cross-track excursions against a 0.200 m budget and (b) the planner's occasional slow, jerky run, which doubles both. | **A PROCESS CORRECTION, recorded rather than hidden**: Flight AY's log directory was OVERWRITTEN by the re-run that this row calls AY2, because `_stage/flightAY.sh` hard-coded its tag and was invoked twice -- the numbers quoted for AY (-0.003 m / 0.06 %) are from its own console output and its figure, which had already been copied into `docs/figures/`, while the directory now holds AY2's (-0.033 m / 0.17 %).  `_stage/flightAZ.sh` takes the tag as an argument for exactly that reason, and the shim now prints its reference conditioning at start-up so a sortie's own log says what it flew. |
| 201 | **A NEW INSTRUMENT PINS THE LAST DEFECT TO A NUMBER, AND IT IS NOT THE TOUR'S TIMING: `_stage/corner-exit-audit.py` walks every leg in the leg's own frame and reports the aircraft's lateral offset at the tour advance and then at 0.25 / 0.50 / 1.00 m down the new edge.  Three sorties (AT clean, AY2 -0.033, AZ -0.238) say the SAME thing on EVERY leg: |across| **p50 0.006-0.032 m at the advance** (the aircraft is on the new line when the shell releases the waypoint), then **+0.18 to +0.26 m at 0.25 m into the leg**, and |across| within 1.5 m of the corner p50 **0.274-0.319 m** (max 0.678).  So the waypoint is NOT released early, the reference is NOT the term, and the excursion is invariant across arms: the aircraft OVERSHOOTS each 90 degree corner by ~0.25 m along the direction it was travelling and then turns back.  That is v x (control lag) -- 1.5 m/s x ~0.15 s = 0.22 m -- against the 0.200 m the field leaves, which is why every contact of the corridor era is a corner and why the number is so reproducible. | **AND BOTH ARMS THE OLD HYPOTHESES SUGGESTED ARE REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT**: Flight BA (`EGO_ARRIVE_M` 0.300 -> **0.150**, i.e. "release the corner later") ABORTED -- the first run on the supervisor's 20 s plan-silence rule (the 0.150 m gate is tight enough that the tour never advanced: "nothing on /plan/cmd within 20.0 s of the trigger"), the second after advancing at 0.139-0.146 m arrivals and then losing the EKF ("EKF Failsafe: changed to Land Mode", A1 381 990 mm, truth box N 9.393) -- and Flight BB (`RECT_HOLD_S` 0.0 -> **0.15 s**, i.e. "let it settle at the corner") is clean on one run (**C2 +0.034 m, 0.00 % inside, C2-ego MET**, A1 9.32 mm, B1 6.202 / 8.381, route 42.0 s) and **-0.290 m / 0.65 % inside** on its repeat, which is the same good-run/bad-run split the default shows (AY -0.003 / AY2 -0.033 / AZ -0.238).  Neither knob moves the corner-exit number, and the audit explains why: at the advance the aircraft is already on the line, so when and how long the tour waits cannot matter.  **WHAT FOLLOWS IS ONE ARITHMETIC STEP**: the overshoot is linear in the speed carried THROUGH the vertex, so 0.70 m/s should put it at ~0.10-0.15 m, inside the budget -- and the seconds the slower corner costs can be paid for by the ceiling, which is why Flight BC is corner **0.70** with `EGO_MAX_VEL`/`ACC` **3.0** (AU's speed, AP's corner). |
| 202 | **CORRECTION TO ROW 201 -- ITS INSTRUMENT READ A GEOMETRIC ARTEFACT, AND THE CORRECTED ONE CHANGES THE DIAGNOSIS: `corner-exit-audit.py` sampled `across` at a fixed DISTANCE FROM THE CORNER, and a point still on the OLD leg 0.25 m before the corner is 0.25 m off the NEW leg's line by construction -- which is why "0.25 m at 0.25 m" looked invariant across every arm.  `_stage/corner-exit-audit2.py` projects onto the new leg's own direction instead and reports the offset at along = 0.25 / 0.50 / 1.00 / 1.50 / 2.00 m plus the worst over the interior (along >= 0.30 m, where the frame is meaningful).  THE REAL NUMBERS: the aircraft's interior |across| is **p50 0.118-0.199 m** and **max 0.20-0.65 m** in every arm flown, i.e. the TYPICAL cross-track error is already the size of the whole 0.200 m budget, and a single bad leg doubles it.  So the corner is not a fixed 0.25 m artefact, it is a distribution whose median sits ON the budget. | **AND THE CORNER SPEED DOES NOT MOVE IT, WHICH THE CORRECTED AUDIT SHOWS AND FLIGHT BC CONFIRMS**: BC (corner `MPS` 1.00 -> **0.70**, radius 0.50, ceiling 3.0) reads interior p50 **0.199** / max **0.479** m against AU's 0.164 / 0.207 -- slightly WORSE -- and its two sorties are C2 **-0.079 m** (0.25 % inside, 6 of 7 legs under 6 s) and **-0.205 m** (0.67 % inside, **7 of 7 legs under 6 s, route 27.5 s**, the fastest sortie of the whole investigation).  The reason is visible in BC2's leg 2: the offset is 0.46 m for the first 1.5 m of the new edge, i.e. a corner taken at speed.  The governor caps the REFERENCE's velocity inside `corner_m`, but the AIRCRAFT is 0.2-0.3 m behind it, so with a 0.50 m zone the aircraft reaches the vertex before it has actually slowed -- the cap acts on the wrong side of the lag. | **THAT IS A TESTABLE PREDICTION**: a WIDE, DEEP corner (`EGO_CORNER_M` 1.00 with `MPS` 0.70) gives the reference a full metre -- 0.7 s at 1.4 m/s -- to brake before the vertex, so the aircraft should arrive slow and the interior offset should fall; the time cost is ~0.5 s per leg and BC2 has 0.64 s of slack on its slowest leg.  Flight BD flies exactly that, twice. |
| 203 | **THE WIDE, DEEP CORNER LOWERS THE TYPICAL CROSS-TRACK ERROR AND STILL DOES NOT BUY CLEARANCE -- and the family is now complete enough to say the corner knobs are not the lever: Flight BD (corner **1.00 m** radius at **0.70 m/s**, ceiling 3.0) reads interior |across| p50 **0.128 m** -- the LOWEST of any arm (AT 0.132, AU 0.164, BC2 0.199) -- and yet C2 minimum **-0.102 m** with **1.00 %** inside, 5.19 % below the 0.10 m standoff, A1 12.84 mm, B1 6.226 / 8.325, legs 7.45 / 4.28 / 4.97 / 4.98 / 5.06 / 4.99 / 8.10 / 4.94 s (6 of 8 under 6 s).  Its REPEAT (BD2) never flew: `ego_planner_node` **died with SIGSEGV (exit -11)** one line after `Triggered! [TRIG]: from INIT to REPLAN_TRAJ`, so the supervisor's 20 s plan-silence rule ended the sortie -- a NEW failure mode, distinct from the SIGABRT executor crash fixed earlier, and it is recorded here with its log rather than guessed about. | **THE CORNER-ZONE FAMILY, all with the corridor at 0.01 m** -- interior p50 |across| and C2 minimum: 0.50 m @ 1.00 m/s + ceiling 2.5 -> 0.132 / **+0.029** (AT); 0.50 @ 1.00 + 3.0 -> 0.164 / -0.007 (AU); 0.50 @ 0.70 + 3.0 -> 0.199 / -0.079 and -0.205 (BC, BC2 -- the fastest sortie of the investigation, route 27.5 s, 7 of 7 legs under 6 s); 1.00 @ 0.70 + 3.0 -> **0.128** / -0.102 (BD).  The corner knobs move the TYPICAL error by +-0.04 m in either direction and the minimum clearance is dominated by the tail, so they are REFUTED as the safety lever -- which is the third independent way of saying what the corrected audit says: **the aircraft's cross-track error (p50 0.12-0.20 m, tail 0.3-0.65 m) is the same size as the whole 0.200 m budget, and no shaping of the reference -- which is now a provably straight line on the field's maximum-clearance path -- can change that.**  What is left is the flight controller's tracking at 1.5-2.0 m/s on a route whose entire margin is 0.2 m, or a route with more room (there is none in this field), or a slower plan (which is exactly the 2.5 m/s default, and it is why the default is 2.5). |
| 204 | **THE POSITION LOOP IS THE LAST LEVER AND IT MOVES THE MECHANISM: `PSC_NE_POS_P` 1.5 -> **1.8** takes the aircraft's interior cross-track error from p50 0.132 m to **p50 0.096-0.130 m** (`_stage/corner-exit-audit2.py`, which is the metric that matters because the budget is 0.200 m) and, flown as a bare default, gives the three best safety sorties of the whole investigation: **Flight BE2 +0.039 m, Flight BF +0.048 m, Flight BF2 +0.056 m** of minimum pillar-surface distance with **0.00 %** of samples inside a body and **C2-ego MET** in all three (Flight BE, the first run of the same arm, is -0.021 m / 0.15 %).  The other numbers at this arm: A1 9.03-10.36 mm, A2 7.33-9.33 mm, B1 E 6.250-6.570 / N 8.254-8.316, C1 **0.008 m** (Flight BF, the tightest final point ever), routes 35.3-40.1 s, legs **7 of 8 under 6 s in BF (max 7.29)** and 6 of 8 in BF2 (max 9.15).  So the safety clause is now met with a POSITIVE, reproducible margin -- the first time that can be said -- and the time clause is the only one left. | **WHY THE OLD REFUTATIONS OF THIS GAIN DO NOT APPLY**: Flight AE raised it TOGETHER with `EGO_REF_ACC_MAX` 2.5, which over-drove the loop (arrivals at 2.16 m/s, A1 58.90 mm), and Flights AX/AX2 flew it with the plan's ceiling at 3.0 (-0.017 / -0.222 m).  At the default's own ceiling (2.5) it had never been flown with the corridor in place, and that is the combination that works: a straight reference gives the proportional term something to hold, and 1.8 s^-1 holds it to 0.10-0.13 m instead of 0.13-0.20 m.  **THE DEFAULT FILE NOW CARRIES 1.8** (`params/guided_limits_fast.parm`, with the measurement written beside it), so a bare `PLANNER=ego` sortie flies it, and Flights BF/BF2 are that bare sortie. |
| 205 | **THE CEILING IS RE-CHECKED WITH THE NEW GAIN AND 2.75 IS STILL WORSE, SO 2.5 STAYS: Flight BG (gain 1.8, `EGO_MAX_VEL`/`ACC` **2.75**) is **-0.273 m** / 0.96 % inside -- a slow-planner run (B1 N 8.687, route 41.4 s) -- and Flight BG2 (the same arm) is **-0.042 m** / 0.26 % inside at A1 9.16 mm, B1 6.186 / 8.275, route 34.6 s.  Against the same arm at 2.5 (BE2 +0.039, BF +0.048, BF2 +0.056, BE -0.021) that is a clear ordering, and it matches every other ceiling experiment of the day: **the clean band ends at 2.5 m/s**, and raising it to buy the last legs under 6 s costs the margin that was just won. | **WHERE THE OBJECTIVE STANDS AT THE END OF ROUND 16** -- defaults: corridor 0.01 m (no opening) + corner 0.50 m / 1.00 m/s + `EGO_MAX_VEL`/`ACC` 2.5 + **`PSC_NE_POS_P` 1.8** + Flight AD's rest.  **The collision clause is MET with a positive margin on three of four bare sorties (+0.039 / +0.048 / +0.056 m, 0.00 % inside, C2-ego MET, worst-instant reference clearance +0.269..+0.284 m).  The time clause is 6-7 of 8 legs under 6 s (max 7.29 s in BF), and every attempt to buy the remaining 1-2 legs -- ceiling 2.75 (BG/BG2), 3.0 (AU/AU2/AS/AW/AX), corner 0.70 (BC/BC2), corner 1.00 x 1.00 m (BD) -- either spends the margin or does nothing.  The two clauses are no longer 0.5 m/s of ceiling apart but ~0.3 m/s, and the residual is the aircraft's cross-track error (p50 0.10-0.13 m of a 0.200 m budget) plus the planner's occasional slow run (which is what turns a graze into -0.27 m).**  Next: the slow run itself is now the biggest single risk -- characterised by route > 41 s, B1 N > 8.5, A1 > 11 mm -- so the next round should find out WHAT makes ego slow occasionally (map occupancy at the first leg, relay window, A* pool) rather than tune the shell further. |
| 206 | **THE LONG LEGS WERE NEVER SLOW -- THEY WERE FROZEN, AND THE FREEZE IS ONE LINE OF THE FSM: `_stage/leg-stall.py` (new this round) reports, per leg, the mean |v_ref|, the fraction of samples below 0.20 m/s and the LONGEST CONTINUOUS stretch below it.  Every leg over 6 s of the default arm contains one: **BF leg 5 26.8 % of the leg still with a 0.75 s freeze, BF2 leg 5 40.4 % with a 3.40 s freeze, BG leg 4 53.4 % with 2.79 s**, all at |v_ref| = 0.00 -- and the FSM's own log carries the matching `[SAFETY]: from EXEC_TRAJ to EMERGENCY_STOP`.  The mechanism is `checkCollisionCallback` (`ego_replan_fsm.cpp:908-913`): when the rest of the committed spline is in collision AND re-committing from the current trajectory fails, it does `if (t - t_cur < emergency_time_) EMERGENCY_STOP` -- and EMERGENCY_STOP publishes a STATIONARY spline, i.e. the reference the shell forwards freezes where the last plan ended -- `else REPLAN_TRAJ`.  `fsm/emergency_time` was **1.0 s in every sortie ever flown** (the node's default, hard-coded in our launch file until today), and flying the centred line 0.2 m from the pillar surfaces makes that branch fire repeatedly. | **`EGO_EMERGENCY_S` 0.0 -- RE-PLAN INSTEAD OF STOP -- AND THE LEGS COME IN: Flight BI and BI2 (the committed defaults plus this one knob) have **ZERO** emergency stops (the FSM log count goes from 4-5 to 0), the total still time per sortie from 1.4-5.4 s to **0.9 / 2.1 s**, and the legs from 7.3-9.2 s to **0.86 / 4.70 / 5.32 / 4.32 / 5.86 / 4.66 / 6.04 / 4.74 s** and **1.33 / 5.38 / 5.44 / 5.05 / 5.64 / 4.60 / 6.59 / 5.99 s** -- **7 of 8 under 6.0 s in both**, with the only misses on the two 8 m east edges (6.04 and 6.59 s).  And the safety did not merely survive, it improved: C2 minimum **+0.020 / +0.057 m** (the best of the whole investigation is BI2's +0.057), 0.00 % inside a body, C2-ego MET in both, A1 9.15 / 9.02 mm, A2 7.18 / 9.51, B1 E 6.211-6.417 / N 8.309-8.442, C1 0.025 / 0.295, routes 35.6 / 38.7 s.  A frozen reference is worse for tracking than a re-plan, which is why removing the stop helps both criteria at once. |
| 207 | **THE CEILING IS REFUTED A FOURTH TIME, NOW WITHOUT THE FREEZE CONFOUND: Flight BJ (BI + `EGO_MAX_VEL`/`ACC` 2.5 -> **2.75**) is **-0.117 m** / 0.39 % inside with B1 E 7.040 / N 8.876 and arrivals at |v| **2.52** and 2.36 m/s, and BJ2 (the same arm) **-0.296 m** / 0.73 % inside with B1 N 9.350 -- i.e. at 2.75 the aircraft starts crossing the corners at speed again, which is exactly the behaviour the corner governor and the 2.5 ceiling were chosen to prevent.  So the arm of record stays `EGO_CORRIDOR_M 0.01` + `EGO_CORNER_M 0.50 / MPS 1.00` + `EGO_MAX_VEL`/`ACC` **2.5** + `PSC_NE_POS_P` **1.8** + **`EGO_EMERGENCY_S` 0.0**, and the objective now stands at **7 of 8 legs under 6 s with 0.00 % of samples inside a body and a positive minimum clearance on two consecutive bare sorties** -- the closest the two clauses have ever been to being met together.  The remaining 0.04-0.59 s live on the two 8 m east edges, and the last seconds have to come from the corner transit (free on safety: AR at corner 0.80 and AT at 1.00 both read +0.03 m) rather than from the ceiling: Flight BK takes `EGO_CORNER_MPS` 1.00 -> 1.10 with everything else at BI. |
| 208 | **THE FRONTIER IS NOW 2 cm AND 0.4 s WIDE, AND BOTH SIDES OF IT ARE MEASURED ON THE IDENTICAL ARM: Flight BK/BK2 (BI with `EGO_CORNER_MPS` 1.00 -> **1.10**) read C2 minimum **-0.020 / -0.018 m** with **0.08 %** of samples inside a body, 0.98 / 1.01 % below the 0.10 m standoff, A1 **8.50** / 9.20 mm, B1 E 6.128-6.266 / N 8.311-8.357, C1 0.099 / 0.070, routes 33.1 / 36.0 s -- and **BK2 flies 8 of 8 legs under 6 s (max 5.95 s)** while BK is 5 of 7 (6.31, 6.30).  Against BI/BI2 (corner 1.00: **+0.020 / +0.057 m**, 0.00 % inside, 7 of 8 legs at 6.04 / 6.59) that makes the trade exact: **+1.10 m/s at the corner buys the last leg-seconds and costs the last two centimetres of clearance.**  And the residual over-time in the CLEAN arm is not speed at all -- BI2's leg 7 sat 0.99 s below 0.20 m/s at (2.99,-3.99), the SE corner, i.e. the same class of stall the emergency window removed from the other legs but at the tour's own arrival gate: the shell releases the next waypoint only when the vehicle is within `EGO_ARRIVE_M` 0.300 m, so the aircraft has to crawl the last centimetres before the reference may move on.  Flights BM (corner 1.00 + `EGO_ARRIVE_M` **0.350**) and BL (corner 1.10 + 0.350) test that in the two directions. | **THE LEDGER OF THE OBJECTIVE, as of this row**: 8 of 8 legs under 6 s -> achieved (BK2, AU, BC2, AW, AX2); 0.00 % of samples inside a body with a POSITIVE minimum -> achieved (BI +0.020, BI2 +0.057, BF +0.048, BE2 +0.039); both in ONE sortie -> still open, by 2 cm or by 0.4 s depending on which corner speed is flown.  Nothing else is left in the arm: the corridor made the reference provably clear, the emergency window removed the freezes, the gain halved the cross-track error, and the ceiling and the corner now trade the last two numbers against each other. |

| 214 | **A HARNESS BUG FOUND BY READING THE LOG INSTEAD OF GUESSING, AND ROW 212's ATTRIBUTION CORRECTED: the three sorties that would not start (BP2, BQ/BQ2, BT/BT2) failed for ONE reason and it was not the arm -- `traj.log`'s first line is argparse's usage and line 23 is `error: argument --approach-via: expected one argument`. | 217 | **THE CROSS-TRACK FEEDBACK IS IMPLEMENTED, SELFTESTED -- AND REFUTED IN FOUR SORTIES: `--xtrack-gain k` shifts the forwarded reference by `-k x e`, where `e` is the AIRCRAFT's own signed offset from the current leg, capped at `--xtrack-max` 0.10 m; the idea was to multiply the position loop's cross-track stiffness by (1 + k) without touching the flight controller, because the corridor had already taken the REFERENCE's error to zero (0.00 % of reference samples inside a pillar since Flight AN) while the aircraft still leaves the line by 0.14-0.36 m.  The selftest covers the three properties that matter (0.200 m offset -> 0.100 m shift, a 0.500 m offset -> the 0.100 m cap, and gain 0 -> byte-identical reference).  **At k = 0.5 it makes things WORSE, on both clauses**: the DEFAULT arm + xtrack reads **-0.276 m** (5 of 8 legs, max 7.24 s) and **-0.031 m** (6 of 7), and the FAST arm + xtrack reads **-0.031 m** (6 of 8, max 6.25) and **-0.046 m** (6 of 8, max 6.94) -- no clean sortie in four, against 3 of 6 clean without it on the same fast arm, and the legs are longer than the arm's own baseline. | **WHY IT CANNOT WORK AS WRITTEN, and it is the same reason the corridor was needed**: the bias is computed from the FCU's own odometry, sample by sample, so it re-injects the estimator's own noise and its own transients into the REFERENCE -- a target that jitters at the odometry's rate is a harder target for the position loop, not an easier one, and the net effect of `-k*e` on a loop that already sees `e` is to make the reference chase the aircraft whenever `e` changes quickly.  A filtered version (or one that biases on the PLAN's own lag rather than on the measurement) is the only form in which this could be retried; as measured, `EGO_XTRACK_GAIN` stays **0.0** and the knob is documented off. | **WHERE ROUND 20 LEAVES THE OBJECTIVE** -- unchanged: the fast arm (corner 1.10, gain 2.0) meets both clauses in **2 of 6** sorties and the safety-first default is clean in **4 of 5** with 6-7 of 8 legs; every shell-side lever now has an evidence trail behind it (corridor, emergency window, position gain, corner speed and radius, ceiling, arrival and re-plan meters, approach way, cross-track feedback), and the residual is the aircraft's 0.14-0.36 m cross-track excursion against the 0.200 m the field leaves, which the shell can lead but cannot stiffen. |

The numbers say the opposite: with a 0.10 m lateral offset the RADIAL gate fires once `hypot(0.10, dz) <= 0.30`, i.e. `dz <= 0.283` -- **the leg already ends 0.28 m BEFORE the corner's plane**, so a plane gate with 0.05 m of slack would end it 0.23 m LATER and make every leg longer.  The knob was added, selftested (its own case proved the radial gate fires first), and removed again: `git status` on the shim is EMPTY this round, the selftest passes, `bash -n` is clean, and the tree carries no trace of the idea beyond this row.  **What that leaves as the true tail**: the DECELERATION into each corner, which is governed by `--corner-mps` 1.10 over `--corner-m` 0.50 -- and both are bracketed on either side by measurement (zone 0.35: -0.257 / -0.175 m; zone 0.65: -0.147 m; speed 1.20: -0.113 m; speed 1.00: clean but 6-7 of 8 legs). |

| 234 | **THE OWNER ASKED WHETHER THE CORNER CAN BE SLOWED AND THE ARRIVAL SPEED TAKEN TO ZERO.  IT CAN, BOTH WERE FLOWN THIS ROUND, AND NEITHER IMPROVES THE CLEARANCE -- THE DEFAULT IS STILL THE BEST OF THE FOUR ON BOTH CLAUSES.**  The two knobs are real and named: `EGO_CORNER_MPS` is the CORNER GOVERNOR (within `EGO_CORNER_M` 0.50 m of the corner the forwarded reference may not exceed it, tapering out at the radius, `ego_contract_shim.py:501-517`) and `EGO_SETTLE_MPS` is the ARRIVAL GATE's speed test (the tour does not advance until `d_xy <= EGO_ARRIVE_M` AND `|v| < settle`, `:203`; **0 switches the test off**, i.e. 'fly through', which is what every Mission-B sortie has flown).  All three flights below are on the maximally random forest, bare arm otherwise.  **(1) `EGO_CORNER_MPS` 1.10 -> 0.50** (FLIGHTCRN050): C2 minimum **-0.168 m**, 7 of 8 legs under 6 s (max 6.05) -- **worse clearance than the default's -0.090 m**, the same direction as every slow-plan result in this investigation (STATUS 200: the two worst sorties of the day were its two slowest).  **(2) `EGO_SETTLE_MPS` 0.0 -> 0.10**, i.e. every corner must be reached at under 0.10 m/s -- a stop (FLIGHTSET010): C2 **-0.105 m**, **2 of 9 legs** under 6 s (max **15.92 s**, route 64.2 s against 37.3), i.e. the mission takes 1.7x as long and still touches.  **(3) the stop with the gate tightened 0.30 -> 0.20 m** (FLIGHTARR020, the planner's own floor: it refuses to plan within 0.20 m of its goal, `planner_manager.cpp:55`): C2 **-0.089 m**, 3 of 9 legs, max **22.51 s**.  **WHY THE STOP DOES NOT HELP, and this is the round's finding**: the arrival rule is RADIAL -- the tour advances when the vehicle is within the gate of the corner POINT -- so the vehicle may be up to the gate's distance SHORT of the corner when it turns, i.e. that far INSIDE the turn, and on this field the corner pillars stand 0.135-0.21 m from the leg lines.  The corner cut is a GEOMETRIC problem (where the turn is allowed to start), not a KINETIC one (how fast the vehicle arrives): every stopping arm still contacts at a corner (100 % of the inside-body samples within 0.6 m of one in flight 3), and the aircraft cannot be asked to be closer than 0.20 m because the planner will not plan there. |
| 235 | **A 2 s HOVER AT EVERY CORNER: THE BEST CLEARANCE AND THE BEST ARRIVALS OF ANY ARM ON THIS FIELD -- AND THE TIME CLAUSE IS DESTROYED BY CONSTRUCTION.**  Owner: "那改成角点悬停2s".  The knob is `RECT_HOLD_S` -> the contract shell's `--hold-s`: after the arrival gate fires, the tour does not advance until that long has passed (`ego_contract_shim.py:_sequence`), and the entry leg is given none.  Flown with `EGO_SETTLE_MPS=0.10` as well, because with the speed test off the dwell timer starts beside a still-moving aircraft -- the settle clause is what makes it a HOVER (arrived AND slow), so this is ONE variable against FLIGHTSET010 (same field, same settle, hold 0 -> 2 s).  **Flight FLIGHTHOLD2**: every corner was actually hovered (`ARRIVED ... |v| 0.06-0.10 m/s, dwelling 2.0 s`, nine times) and the arrivals became **0.027-0.115 m** against 0.28-0.30 m for the fly-through arm -- a 7x improvement, because a slow arrival does not overshoot the point.  C2 minimum **-0.057 m**, the best of any arm on this field (fly-through -0.090, stop-without-hover -0.105, corner 0.50 -0.168); 323 of 145 342 samples inside a body, and the remaining contact is at **(-3.22, +3.46), 0.22 m OUTSIDE the west leg**, at the NW corner on the second lap, at 1.8 m altitude -- i.e. it is the OUTWARD overshoot as the aircraft leaves the corner, not the inward cut.  B1 E 6.709 / N 8.252 (the N extent is the best of the four arms).  **The cost is the whole time clause**: legs 2.15-10.21 s, **1 of 9 under 6 s**, route 67.7 s against 37.3 -- the 2 s dwell sits inside the leg that follows it, so every leg carries 2 s before it even moves, and no tuning can recover that.  So the hover is a genuine safety lever (the first that has moved C2 on this field) at a known, structural price; it is NOT promoted to the default here -- `RECT_HOLD_S` stays 0.0 until the owner says otherwise. |

The owner's question ("检查起始的yaw是否向着y正方向") produced a mismatch, so it was measured with a CONTROL instead of in isolation: the same test -- the reference's own heading in W (`atan2(d cmd_y, d cmd_x)` on the L reference, rotated by +90.05 deg) against `yaw_cmd` on the wire -- run on two GOOD Mission-B sorties as well as on Mission C.  Results (median / p10 / p90 of yaw - heading): Mission C r14 **-152.9 / -177.9 / +173.6 deg**; Mission B `FLIGHTBARE-defaults` **-103.3 / -178.1 / +179.0 deg**; Mission B `FLIGHTHOLD2` **+19.9 / -178.4 / +179.4 deg**.  The spread is the whole circle in EVERY mission, so the wire's yaw does not encode the reference's heading anywhere in this stack -- it is an inconsistency that Mission B has always lived with, not the cause of Mission C's failures.  **And the aircraft ignores it**: the truth's yaw is +0.0 deg for the whole of every sortie measured (r14, r3), i.e. the FCU never adopts the commanded heading in this GUIDED configuration.  Two consequences, both stated rather than assumed: (1) the path will NOT change by fixing the yaw -- a multirotor's position loop is yaw-independent -- so it must not be sold as a fix for Mission C's instability; (2) the ONLY thing it can change is the LiDAR's view, and only if the FCU is first made to ADOPT the heading, which it currently does not.  The round therefore closes the owner's item honestly: the aircraft points +y at the start, the wire does not carry the heading, nothing follows it, and the next step is to make the FCU hold the commanded yaw (check what the bridge writes into the MAVLink yaw field against `ATT.Yaw` in `flight.BIN`) before any claim that turning would help the estimator. |
| 252 | **ROUND 14: THE AIRCRAFT DOES TURN -- TO **188 deg**, I.E. SOUTH, AND HOLDS IT -- AND MY EARLIER "IT NEVER TURNED" WAS A BAD EXTRACTION.  Round 13 read the truth's yaw out of the tap's quaternion and got +0.0 deg for the whole flight, and concluded the FCU ignored the heading command.  The FCU's own log says otherwise: `flight.BIN`'s **`ATT.Yaw`** (centi-degrees) in r14 is **4.9 cd at 31.4 s then 18 845-20 528 cd from 36 s to the end** -- the vehicle turned to **~188 deg** immediately after take-off and held it.  My extraction was the bug: the tap's quaternion is not being interpreted in the FCU's heading sense, so the number I computed was meaningless and the conclusion built on it ("the FCU ignores the heading, so fixing yaw cannot change anything") is withdrawn.  **What stands, and it is worth more**: the bridge DOES pass the yaw (mask `POS_VEL_ACC_YAW = IGNORE_YAW_RATE`, `mavlink_impl.py:57,453,469`), the FCU DOES adopt a heading, and the heading it adopts is **~188 deg while the mission runs north (0 deg)** -- a ~180 deg error, which is exactly the kind of thing that makes the LiDAR look at the forest from the wrong side for the whole flight.  So the owner's instinct ("check the starting yaw against +y") is vindicated on the FCU's data, not on mine: the aircraft is commanded and flown pointing SOUTH.  **Next round's single change is therefore in the contract's yaw**: find the sign/frame error between the plan's L yaw and the FCU's heading so that `ATT.Yaw` sits near 0 deg for a northward tour, verify it on the log (`ATT.Yaw` after the trigger), and only then fly Mission C again with LIO. |
| 253 | **ROUND 15: THE YAW DEFECT IS IN ONE LINE OF `mavlink_impl.send_cmd` -- AND r14's 188 deg TURNS OUT TO BE THE BAD PLAN'S OWN HEADING PASSED THROUGH, NOT A SEPARATE BUG.  The code: `send_cmd` converts the POSITION with the calibration (`p_f = frames.l_to_wire(cmd_l.p, calib)`) and then does `yaw = float(cmd_l.yaw ...)` and hands it **unconverted** to `set_position_target_local_ned_send` (`src/bridge/mavlink_impl.py:453,469`), whose yaw field is the FCU's COMPASS HEADING (0 = North, + East, `cmd_stream.py:83-89`).  The plan's yaw is an **L-frame angle**, so the wire needs `theta_W = theta_L + yaw_calib` followed by the ENU-angle -> compass map `psi = pi/2 - theta_W` -- two operations that are simply absent.  This is the SAME class of defect the file already documents for the odometry quaternion two hundred lines below ("sending q_f unconverted made the FCU believe a North-facing vehicle faced EAST", `send_external_nav`'s docstring, measured 2026-10-13); it was fixed there and never here.  **And what r14's 188 deg actually was**: the plan's own yaw sits near -pi for most of that flight (range +-pi in `traj.csv.cmd.csv`), which passed through unconverted means 'compass -180 deg' = south -- and the plan WAS flying south, because that is the direction its degenerate trajectory went.  So the aircraft faithfully held the heading of a plan that was leaving the world; the yaw chain did not add an error to a good plan, the plan itself was pointing the wrong way.  Both statements belong together: the L->compass conversion is missing (to be fixed, with a unit test at +-90 deg where the difference is unambiguous), and it is NOT what has been wrecking Mission C. |
